3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.12856pt\raise-33.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 52.12856pt\raise-33.00946pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Lemma 6.3
.

We have the following properties of the flows:

(1)

The critical manifold of the flow
\psi_{z}^{1}
are the cores

C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{0}

and the stable manifold for
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
is the
strata
S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(2)

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
, we denote by

\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}=\{\gamma\in{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|\underset{z\rightarrow 0}{\lim}\ \psi^{1}_{z}(\gamma)\in C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\}

the corresponding unstable manifold.
We have
\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}|_{0}\simeq T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
for

\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
. The isomorphism

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{x}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x},\ x\in{\mathcal{H}}
,
restricts to an isomorphism

\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}|_{x}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

for
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}

Proof.

This is proved in
[
N1
, Proposition 8.4]
.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

(1)

For any
\gamma\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}
,
the map
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})},\ z\to\psi_{z}^{1}(\gamma)

given by the flow
\psi_{z}^{1}

extends to a map
a_{\gamma}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}
such that

a_{\gamma}(0)=\underset{z\to 0}{\lim}\ \psi_{z}^{1}(\gamma)
.

(2)

For any
\mathcal{E}\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
,
the action map
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})),\ z\to\psi_{z}^{2}(\mathcal{E})

given by the flow
\psi_{z}^{2}

extends to a map

(6.11)

a_{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})).

Moreover, we have

a_{\mathcal{E}}(z)\simeq\mathcal{E}
for all
z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
, and
for any
\gamma\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}
,
there is a commutative
diagram

(6.12)

\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{a_{\gamma}}
\scriptstyle{a_{\mathcal{E}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}

where
\mathcal{E}=q(\gamma)\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Lemma
6.3
(2).
Proof of part (2).
Let
\gamma\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and let

\mathcal{E}=q(\gamma)\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
. Consider the
the composed map

a_{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a_{\gamma}}}{{\to}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))

where
a_{\gamma}
is the map in part (1) and the last isomorphism is the real uniformization (see Prop.
6.2
).
It is elementary to check that the map

a_{\mathcal{E}}
only depends on
\mathcal{E}
and

a_{\mathcal{E}}(z)=\psi_{z}^{2}(\mathcal{E})
for
z\in{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
, hence
defines the desired map in (
6.11
). Moreover,
since
G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])
-orbits
T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
are unstable manifolds for the flow

\psi_{z}^{1}
, we have
a_{\gamma}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0})\subset T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
if
\gamma\in T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
, and it implies

a_{\mathcal{E}}(z)\simeq\mathcal{E}
for all
a\in{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
.
The commutativity of diagram (
6.12
) follows from
the construction of
a_{\mathcal{E}}
.

∎

Proposition 6.5
.

Let
x\in{\mathcal{H}}
.
The complex uniformization
u_{x,{\mathbb{C}}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))

induces an isomorphism

LG_{x,{\mathbb{R}}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{}\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))

of semi-analytic stacks.

Proof.

Let
\gamma\in{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}

and
let
\mathcal{E}=u_{x,{\mathbb{C}}}(\gamma)\in\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))

be the image of the complex uniformization map. By Lemma
6.4
(2)
we have

(6.13)

|\mathcal{E}|=|a_{\mathcal{E}}(0)|=|q(a_{\gamma}(0)|=|u_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}(\underset{z\to 0}{\lim}\ \psi_{z}^{1}(\gamma))|,

(6.14)

\underset{z\to 0}{\lim}\ \psi_{z}^{1}({\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{x})=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}.

As the image
\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}|q(C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})|
of the critical manifolds under

u_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}

is equal to
|\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))|
, equations (
6.13
) and (
6.14
) imply that

u_{x,{\mathbb{C}}}
factors through
u_{x,{\mathbb{C}}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
and induces a surjection
between the sets of isomorphism classes of objects.
Now a similar argument as in the proof Proposition
6.2
shows that

u_{x,{\mathbb{C}}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
is surjective and hence
an isomorphism
LG_{x,{\mathbb{R}}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{x}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
.
∎

6.3.
Multi-point uniformization

We can also consider multi-point uniformization

u^{(n)}:{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{{\mathbb{G}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})\times({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n},\ \ (x,\mathcal{E},\phi)\to(\mathcal{E},x)

which
exhibit
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
as a
LG^{(n)}
-torsor over
\operatorname{Bun}_{{\mathbb{G}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})\times({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}
.
Assume
n=2m
.
the map
u^{(2n)}
induces a map

u^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{\mathbb{R}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{{\mathbb{G}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}\times({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}

on the corresponding real analytic stacks.
Then the same discussions in the previous sections apply to the multi-points setting and we have the following:

Proposition 6.6
.

The map
u^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{\mathbb{R}}

induces an isomorphism

LG_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))\times({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}.

of semi-analytic stacks.

7.
Quasi-maps and Quillen’s homeomorphism

In this section we
study the stack of quasi-maps and its real forms
and use them to provide moduli interpretation for
the quotient

LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
. Using our previous work
[
CN
]
,
we show that
the
family of real quasi-maps is topologically trivial
and deduce from it a multi-point version and refinement of
of Quillen result
[
M
]
on homotopy equivalences
between
loop spaces of symmetric varieties and real affine Grassmannians.

7.1.
Definition of quasi-maps

For
n\geq 0
, define the stack of quasi-maps with poles

QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
to classify triples
(z,{\mathcal{E}},\sigma)
comprising a point
z=(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})\in({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}
, a
G
-torsor
{\mathcal{E}}
over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, and a section
\sigma:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus|z|\to\mathcal{E}\times^{G}X
where we write
|z|=\cup_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\subset{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.
According to
[
GN1
]
,
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
is an ind-stack of ind-finite type.
Note the natural maps
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)\to({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}
and
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)\to\operatorname{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
.
Let
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)
be the ind-scheme classifies
quadruple
(z,\mathcal{E},\phi,\iota)
where
z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
,

\mathcal{E}
is a
G
-bundle on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
\phi:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}-|z|\rightarrow\mathcal{E}\times^{G}X
, and

\iota:\mathcal{E}_{K}|_{\infty}\simeq K
, here
\mathcal{E}_{K}
is the
K
-reduction of

\mathcal{E}
on
C-|z|
given by
\phi
. We have a natural map

QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
.
The ind-scheme
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
is called rigidified quasi-maps.
Note that we have natural map

QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)\to QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{n}}

sending
(z,\mathcal{E},\phi,\iota)
to
(z,\mathcal{E},\phi)

and it induces an isomorphism

(7.1)

K\backslash QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)\simeq QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{n}}

where the group
K
acts on

QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)

by changing the trivialization
\iota
.
For any
z\in({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}
(resp.
z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
), we denote by

QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},z,X)
(resp.
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},z,X,\infty)
)
the fiber at
z
.

7.2.
Real forms of quasi-maps

Let
n=2m
.
The twisted conjugation

\sigma_{2m}
on
({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2m}
together with the involution

\eta
on
G
defines an involution on

QM^{(2m)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
and we denote by

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{\mathbb{R}}
the corresponding semi-analytic stack.
Note there are natural maps

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{\mathbb{R}}\to({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}\text{\ \ \ \ \ }QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{\mathbb{R}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}.

Since
c(\infty)=\infty
,
we have
a real form of

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}
of
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)
.
We have natural maps

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{m}\text{\ \ \ \ \ }QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}\to\operatorname{Bun}_{\mathbb{G}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}.

7.3.
Morphisms

Let
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
be two reductive groups with
complex conjugations
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
and Cartan involutions
\theta_{1}

and
\theta_{2}
respectively.
Then the constructions of quasi-maps, rigidified quasi-maps, uniformization
morphisms, and real forms of those are functorial with respect to
homomorphism

f:G_{1}\to G_{2}
that intertwine

\eta_{1},\eta_{2}
and
\theta_{1},\theta_{2}
.

7.4.
Uniformizations of quasi-maps

We have a natural uniformization map

(7.2)

q^{(n)}:{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\to QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)

exhibits

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
as
a
LK^{(n)}
-torsor on
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
.
In particular, there is a canonical isomorphism of ind-stacks

(7.3)

LK^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X).

Assume
n=2m
.
The morphism in (
7.2
)
gives rise to a uniformization map

q^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{\mathbb{R}}:{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\longrightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{\mathbb{R}}

on the corresponding semi-algebraic stacks of real points.
The following proposition follows from Proposition
6.6
:

Proposition 7.1
.

The uniformization map
q^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{\mathbb{R}}
induces an isomorphism

LK^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}.

Example 7.2
.

By Lemma
5.2
and
Proposition
5.4
,
we have

LK_{c}^{(\sigma_{2m})}|_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})^{m}}:=L^{-}K_{c}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}}\simeq K_{c}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{m}

,
LK_{c}^{(\sigma_{2m})}|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}\simeq LK_{c}^{m}

and
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}\simeq\Omega G_{c}^{(m)}

and hence there are isomorphisms

(7.4)

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}\simeq LK_{c}^{(m)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathcal{H}}^{m}}\simeq LK_{c}^{(m)}\backslash\Omega G_{c}^{(m)}

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})^{m}}\simeq K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{\mathbb{R}}

7.5.
Quillen’s homeomorphism

We fist recall some results in
[
CN1
]

on quasi-maps for complex groups.

Consider the
complex group
G\simeq G\backslash(G\times G)
viewed as a symmetric variety for
G\times G
.
It corresponds to the swap involution
\operatorname{sw}(g_{1},g_{2})=(g_{2},g_{1})

of

G\times G
and
the corresponding conjugation on
G\times G
is given by

\operatorname{sw}_{c}(g_{1},g_{2})=(\eta_{c}(g_{2}),\eta_{c}(g_{1}))
.
Consider the real quasi-maps space

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{m}

associated to
G\backslash(G\times G)
.

The natural projections

p_{1},p_{2}:G\times G\to G

induces natural isomorphism

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G\times G}^{(2m)}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2m}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}.

Consider the base changes

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}
and
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}_{G\times G}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}

along the embedding
({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2m},(z_{1},...,z_{m})\to(z_{1},...,z_{m},\bar{z}_{1},...,\bar{z}_{m})
.

We have a natural embedding

(7.5)

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}

sending
(z\in({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m},\mathcal{E},\phi)\to((z,\bar{z})\in({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2m},\mathcal{E},\phi|_{{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus z\cup\bar{z}})
.
Note that
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{G\times G,{\mathbb{R}}}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}_{G\times G}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}
and
it was proved in
[
CN
, Corollary 5.2 and Corollary 5.4]

that
the natural projection map

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}_{G\times G}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\simeq}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2m}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}

restricts to an isomorphism

(7.6)

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{G\times G,{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}

and the composed map

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\ref{splitting}}}{{\to}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2m)}|_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{m}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle~\eqref{projection}}}{{\simeq}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{G\times G,{\mathbb{R}}}

induces a real analytic isomorphism

(7.7)

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m}}\simeq\Omega G_{c}^{(\sigma_{2m})}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2m})}_{G\times G,{\mathbb{R}}}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{m}}\simeq QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}.

Let

{\mathbb{S}}:={\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}
and viewed as a subset of

{\mathbb{C}}^{m}
via the embedding

(7.8)

{\mathbb{S}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{m},\ \ \ (b_{1},...,b_{m},ia)\to(b_{1}+ia,...,b_{m}+ia)

We now consider the base change of the isomorphism (
7.7
) to
{\mathcal{S}}
:

(7.9)

v:{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathcal{S}}\simeq QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}.

The conjugation
\eta\times\eta

on
G\times G
commutes with
\operatorname{sw}_{c}
, together with
the complex conjugation on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, it
defines an involution
(\eta\times\eta)^{(\sigma_{2m})}

on
QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}

and, via the isomorphism
v_{\mathcal{S}}
, it induces an involution

\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathbb{S}}
.

The following description of
\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}
is proved in
[
CN1
, Proposition 5.12]
.

Proposition 7.3
.

The involution
\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathbb{S}}
is given by the following formula:

\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}=\eta^{(m)}\text{\ \ \ \ on\ \ \ \ }\ \ {\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}

\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}=\tilde{\theta}\text{\ \ \ \ \ \ on\ \ \ \ }\ \ {\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}\simeq\Omega G_{c}^{(2m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}.

Denote by

(7.10)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}:=({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathcal{S}})^{\eta^{(\sigma_{2m})}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}}

the fixed-points.
Then the isomorphism (
7.9
)
restricts to natural isomorphisms

(7.11)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.02676pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.02676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{v_{0}:{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.90176pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.02676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.02676pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)})^{\eta^{(m)}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}={\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{\mathbb{R}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}

\textstyle{v_{a\neq 0}:{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{ia\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{(\Omega G_{c}^{(m)})^{\tilde{\theta}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{ia\}}\simeq\Omega X_{c}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{ia\}}}

The spherical strata
S^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}

for
\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}:{\mathfrak{p}}\to\Lambda_{T}^{+}
(resp. cospherical
T^{(1),\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(1)}

for

\lambda\in\Lambda_{T}^{+}
) give rise to spherical strata

{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}=S^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}

of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}

(resp. cospherical strata

{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}=T^{(1),\lambda}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}
of
{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}
).
Then the isomorphisms (
7.11
)
restrict to

(7.12)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.72528pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.72528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ {\mathcal{Q}}^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\times\{0\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 44.60028pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.72528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.72528pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\times\{0\}}\ \ \ \ (resp.\ \ \ \ {\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}|_{{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 253.64594pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 271.77094pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 271.77094pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{T^{(1),\lambda}_{{\mathbb{R}}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces|_{{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}})

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 32.81567pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-32.81567pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\times ia}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 38.69067pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.81567pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 56.81567pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{P^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\times\{ia\}}\ \ \ (resp.\ \ {\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}|_{{\mathbb{R}}\times{ia}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 230.19861pt\raise 4.34428pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 248.32361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 248.32361pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Q^{(1),\lambda}|_{{\mathbb{R}}\times\{ia\}}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces)

As
S_{\mathbb{R}}^{(m),\lambda}
(resp.
T^{(1),\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}
) is non-empty if and only if
\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}:{\mathfrak{p}}\to\Lambda_{A}^{+}\subset\Lambda_{T}^{+}

(resp.

\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
), the same applies to the stratum

{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}
).
We have the following:

Proposition 7.4
.

Equip

{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}
(resp.
{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}
)
and
{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}
with the stratifications

\{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m),{\mathfrak{p}}_{\lambda}}\}
(resp.
\{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1),\lambda}\}
or
\{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(1)}_{\lambda}\}
)
and
\{{\mathbb{R}}^{\mathfrak{p}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\}_{{\mathfrak{p}}\in p(m)}
respectively.
There is a
K_{c}
-equivariant stratified trivilization of the family

{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}\to{\mathbb{S}}={\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times i{\mathbb{R}}

over
i{\mathbb{R}}
.

Proof.

Since the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{S}}
is ind-proper and
there is a
K_{c}
-equivariant trivialization

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathcal{S}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}
over
i{\mathbb{R}}

the desired claim follows from the general lemma in
[
CN1
, Lemma 6.6]
.

∎

Now the homeomorphisms in (
7.11
) and (
7.12
) together with
Proposition
5.6
and Proposition
7.4
imply the following

Theorem 7.5
.

There are
K_{c}
-equivariant strata-preserving homeomorphisms

\Omega K_{c}^{(m)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{i\}}\simeq\Omega X_{c}^{(m)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{i\}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{m}\times\{0\}}

which restrict to
K_{c}
-equivariant homeomorphisms

\Omega K_{c}^{(m)}\backslash{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{(n),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq P^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}\simeq S^{(m),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}_{\mathbb{R}}

\Omega K_{c}^{(1)}\backslash{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{(1),\lambda}\simeq P^{(1),\lambda}\simeq S_{\mathbb{R}}^{(1)}\ \ \ \ \ \Omega K_{c}^{(1)}\backslash{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(1),\lambda}\simeq Q^{(1),\lambda}\simeq T^{(1),\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}\ \ \text{if}\ \ m=1

7.6.
Trivialization of real quasi-maps

Consider the morphism
f:G\to G\times G,\ g\to(g,\theta(g))
.
It is equivariant for the conjugations and Cartan involutions on

G
and
G\times G
hence, by the functoriality noted in Section
7.3
, we obtain a map

(7.13)

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{v}}}{{\simeq}}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}|_{\mathcal{S}}.

Lemma 7.6
.

The map (
7.13
) restricts to a
K_{c}
-equivariant homeomorphism

\textstyle{QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{{\mathcal{S}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}}

In particular,
QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{S}}
is topologically trivial
over
i{\mathbb{R}}

Proof.

According to Example
7.2

and (
7.11
),
there are natural identifications

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R},{\mathcal{L}}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{{\mathbb{R}}},\ \ \ \ \ \ {\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{\mathbb{R}}\text{\ \ \ \ \ over\ \ \ \ }{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}

QM^{(\sigma_{2m})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R},{\mathcal{L}}}\simeq\Omega K_{c}^{(m)}\backslash\Omega G_{c}^{(m)},\ \ \ \ \ \ {\mathcal{Q}}^{(m)}\simeq\Omega X_{c}^{(m)}\text{\ \ \ \ \ over\ \ \ \ }{\mathbb{R}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}

and under the above identifications the map (
7.13
)
specializes to the natural inclusion map

(7.14)

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(m)}}
\textstyle{\text{over\ \ \ \ }{\mathbb{R}}\times\{0\}}

and
to the natural map

(7.15)

\textstyle{\Omega K_{c}^{(m)}\backslash\Omega G_{c}^{(m)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega X^{(m)}_{c}}
\textstyle{\text{over\ \ \ \ }{\mathbb{R}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}}

The lemma follows from Proposition
5.6
.

∎

We have a flow
\psi_{z},z\in{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}

on

(7.16)

\psi_{z}:QM^{(2)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)\rightarrow QM^{(2)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)

\ \psi_{z}((x,\mathcal{E},\psi,\iota))=(a_{z}(x),(a_{z^{-1}})^{*}\mathcal{E},(a_{z^{-1}})^{*}\psi,\iota).

8.
Real-symmetric equivalence

In this section we construct the real-symmetric equivalence
between the real and relative Satake categories.
The theory of sheaves on infinite dimensional stacks
developed in
[
BKV
]
plays an important role here.

8.1.
Placid stacks

We first review the notion of placid (ind)-schemes and (ind)-stacks.
Let
Y
be a scheme
acted on by an affine group scheme
H
.
we say that

Y
is
H
-
placid
if

•

Y
can be written as filtered limit
Y=\lim_{j}Y_{j}
,
where each
Y_{j}
is a
H
-scheme of finite type and the transition maps

Y_{j^{\prime}}\to Y_{j}
for
j\to j^{\prime}
are affine, smooth, surjective, and
H
-equivariant.

•

the action
H\times Y_{j}\to Y_{j}
factors through
a group scheme
H_{j}
of finite type. Moreover
the
H_{j}
can be chosen so that
\{H_{j}\}_{j}
forms a projective
system with
H=\operatorname{lim}_{j}H_{j}
and the transition maps
H_{j^{\prime}}\to H_{j}

are smooth surjective with
unipotent
kernel.

Let
Z\subset Y
a
H
-invariant subscheme.
We shall say that the inclusion
Z\to Y

is placid
if there is a presentation of
Y=\operatorname{lim}Y_{j}
as above and
and index
j
and a
H
-invariant subscheme
Z_{j}\subset Y_{j}

such that

Z\cong Z_{j}\times_{Y_{j}}Y
.

Let
Y
be an ind-scheme acted on by an affine group scheme
H
.
We say that
Y
is
H
-ind placid if can be written as filtered colimit

(8.1)

Y\cong\operatorname{colim}_{i}Y^{i}

where each
Y^{i}
is
H
-placid
and the transition maps
Y^{i}\to Y^{i^{\prime}}
are
placid closed embeddings.
A presentation as in (
8.1
)
is called a placid presentation of
Y
.

We call a stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
a placid stack (resp.
ind-placid stack) if it is isomorphic to a stack of the form

{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq Y/H
where
Y
is
H
-placid scheme (resp.
H
-ind placid scheme).

We recall the following basic result due to Drinfeld and
Bouthier:

Proposition 8.1
.

(1)

[
D
, Theorem 6.3]
and
[
B
, Proposition 2.0.1]

For any smooth affine
{\mathbb{C}}
-scheme
Y
of finite type, the loop space

Y({\mathcal{K}})
is ind-placid.

(2)

[
D
, Proposition 3.8]
and

[
B
, Proposition 1.1.4]
Let
G
be a complex connected reductive group and let

H\subset G
be a connected reductive subgroup.
The natural map
G({\mathcal{K}})\to(G/H)({\mathcal{K}})
is a

H({\mathcal{K}})
-torsor in the
h
-topology.
In particular, there is an isomorphism of stacks

G({\mathcal{K}})/H({\mathcal{K}})\simeq(G/H)({\mathcal{K}})

Remark 8.2
.

Part (2) above is equivalent to the following fact:
Let
A
be a
{\mathbb{C}}
-algebra.
Any
G
-torsor on
\operatorname{Spec}(A((t)))
is trivial over

\operatorname{Spec}(B((t)))

for a
h
-cover
\operatorname{Spec}(B)\to\operatorname{Spec}(A)
.
This fact is claimed in
[
D
, Proposition 3.8, Remark (b)]

with sketch of proof.
In
[
B
]
, the author provided a different proof.

In
[
CY
, Proposition 26]
,
we deduce
the following results from Proposition
8.1
(1).
4
4

4

In
loc. cit.
we only deal with the case of classical symmetric varieties
using an explicit construction of (ind)-plaicd presentations.
The proof of the general case, using Proposition
8.1
, will appear in the revised version of the paper.

Proposition 8.3
.

(1)
X({\mathcal{K}})
is
G({\mathcal{O}})
-ind placid.
(2) The orbits closure
\overline{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}}
is
G({\mathcal{O}})
-placid.
(3) The inclusion
X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}\to\overline{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}}

is placid.

Proposition
8.1
(2) immediately implies

Corollary 8.4
.

(1) The stack
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
is ind-placid.
(2) The stack
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}

is placid and the inclusion
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}^{\lambda}_{K}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}
is placid.
(3) There is an isomorphism of ind-placid stacks
X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})\simeq K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
and isomorphisms of placid stacks

K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\overline{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}\simeq\overline{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}}/G({\mathcal{O}})
.

8.2.
Stacks admitting gluing of sheaves

We recall the notion of stacks admitting gluing of sheaves in
[
BKV
, Section 5.5]
.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be stack and let

\eta:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a locally closed embedding of finite presentation (fp-locally closed embedding for short).
According to
[
BKV
, Section 5.4.4]
, there are well-defined continuous functor

\eta_{*}:D({\mathcal{S}})\to D({\mathcal{Y}})

Definition 8.5
.

We say a stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
admits gluing of sheaves
if for every fp-locally closed embedding
\eta:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

the functors
\eta_{*}:D({\mathcal{S}})\to D({\mathcal{Y}})
admits a left adjoint
\eta^{*}:D({\mathcal{Y}})\to D({\mathcal{S}})
.

We have following basic facts:

Lemma 8.6
.

[
BKV
, Lemma 5.5.3]

Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a stack admitting gluing of sheaves.
(1) Let
\eta:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a fp-locally closed embedding.
There exists a left adjoint
\eta_{!}
of
\eta^{!}:D({\mathcal{S}})\to D({\mathcal{X}})

(2) Let
j:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a fp-open embedding and
i:{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}

be the complementary fp-closed embedding.
For every
\mathcal{F}\in D(X)
there is a fiber sequence

j_{!}j^{!}\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}i^{*}\mathcal{F}.

Lemma 8.7
.

[
BKV
, Proposition 5.5.7]

Let
H
be an ind-placid group, that is, a group object in
ind-placid schemes, acting on an ind-placid scheme
Y
.
The quotient stack
Y/H
admits gluing of sheaves.

Example 8.8
.

Let
H
be a complex affine group scheme of finite type acting on
a complex smooth affine scheme
Y
of finite type.
Then the loop group
H({\mathcal{K}})

is an ind-placid group and the loop space
Y({\mathcal{K}})
is an ind-placid scheme, and
Lemma
8.7
implies the
quotients

H({\mathcal{K}})\backslash Y({\mathcal{K}})

admits glueing of sheaves.

The following technical lemma
will be used in Section
12.2
.

Definition 8.9
.

Let
f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a morphism between stacks.
We say

f
is
strongly pro-smooth
is
there is a surjective morphism

Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
such that the
pullback
f_{Y}:X:={\mathcal{X}}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}Y\to Y
satisfying the following:
there is a presentation
X\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{i\in I}X_{i}

as filtered limit of ind-stacks ind-locally of finite type
over
Y
such that all the projection maps

X_{i}\to Y
are fp-smooth morphisms
and the transition maps
X_{i}\to X_{i^{\prime}}

are fp-affine smooth morphisms.

Lemma 8.10
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}
is an ind-stack of ind-locally of finite type.

(1)

{\mathcal{X}}
admits gluing of sheaves.

(2)

Let
f:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a strongly pro-smooth morphism.
Then
{\mathcal{Y}}
admits gluing of sheaves and
for any fp-locally closed embedding
\eta:{\mathcal{S}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
the
base change morphism

\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{*}f^{!}\to f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}\eta^{*}
(resp.
(\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}})_{!}f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}\to f^{!}\eta_{!}
),
is isomorphism.
Here
f_{\mathcal{S}}
and

\eta_{\mathcal{S}}
are the natural projection maps in the following fiber product

\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\mathcal{S}}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathcal{S}}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta}
\textstyle{\mathcal{Y}}

Proof.

Choose a presentation

(8.2)

{\mathcal{X}}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\beta}{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\beta\in I}\operatorname{colim}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}

as a filtered colimit,
where
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}
are stacks
of finite type and the transition maps
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
(resp.
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha^{\prime}}
)
are closed embeddings of finite presentation (resp. open embeddings of finite presentation).
It follows from
[
BKV
, Example 1.3.4]
that

{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}
is
placid
in the sense of
[
BKV
, Section 1.3]

and
[
BKV
, Lemma 5.5.5, and Lemma 5.5.6]
implies

{\mathcal{X}}
admits glueing of sheaves.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}
(resp.
{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{[n]}
)
be the terms of the
\check{\operatorname{C}}
ech complex associated to

f
(resp.
f_{\mathcal{S}}
)
and let
\eta^{[n]}_{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{X}}_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{[n]}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}

pullback of
\eta_{S}
along the projection map

{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}\to{\mathcal{X}}
.
We claim that

(1)

each
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}

admits glueing of sheaves

(2)

for every natural projection

f:{\mathcal{X}}^{[m]}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}
, with pullback
f_{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{[m]}\to{\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{[n]}
,
there is a natural isomorphism

f^{!}(\eta^{[n]}_{\mathcal{S}})_{*}\simeq(\eta^{[m]}_{\mathcal{S}})_{*}f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}

(3)

(Beck-Chevalley condition)
For every projection
f
as in (2),
there is a natural isomorphism

(8.3)

(\eta^{[m]}_{\mathcal{S}})^{*}f^{!}\simeq f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}(\eta^{[n]}_{\mathcal{S}})^{*}\ \ \ \

Now it follows from
[
BKV
, Proposition 5.1.8]
that
the functor
\eta_{*}:D({\mathcal{S}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{[n]}D({\mathcal{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}^{[n]})\to D({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{[n]}D({\mathcal{X}}^{[n]})
admits a left adjoint
\eta^{*}
satisfying the
base change morphism
\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}}^{*}f^{!}\simeq f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}\eta^{*}
.
The lemma follows.

Proof of the claim.
Claim (2) follows from
[
BKV
, Lemma 5.4.5]
.
Note that the presentation (
8.2
) of
{\mathcal{X}}
induces a presentation

{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\beta}{\mathcal{X}}^{[n],\beta}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\beta}\operatorname{colim}_{\alpha}{\mathcal{X}}^{[n],\beta}_{\alpha}

where
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n],\beta}_{\alpha}={\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}\times_{{\mathcal{X}}}{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}
.
The assumption that
f
is strongly pro-smooth implies that the projection
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n],\beta}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}

is
smooth
in the sense of
[
BKV
, Section 1.3.1 (c)]
and, since
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}
is
placid
,
it follows from

[
BKV
, Section 1.3.3 (d), Lemma 5.5.5, and Lemma 5.5.6]
that
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n],\beta}_{\alpha}

is
plaicd
and
{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}
admits glueing of sheaves.
Claim (1) follows.

To show claim (3), we first assume the support of
\mathcal{F}
is contained in some
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}
.
Then the desired claim follows from
[
BKV
, Proposition 5.3.9]
.
Since every object
\mathcal{F}\in D({\mathcal{X}})
can be written as a colimit

\mathcal{F}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{\alpha}\operatorname{colim}_{\beta}\mathcal{F}^{\beta}_{\alpha}

with respect to
!
-pushforward along open embeddings
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}_{\alpha^{\prime}}

and closed embeddings
{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta}\to{\mathcal{X}}^{\beta^{\prime}}
(see, e.g.,
[
BKV
, Corollary 5.1.5]
), the general case follows from the fact that
the functors in (
8.3
) commutes with colimits.

The proof of the base change isomorphism

(\eta_{{\mathcal{S}}})_{!}f_{\mathcal{S}}^{!}\to f^{!}\eta_{!}
is similar. The only non-trivial part is the
the Beck-Chevalley condition
in (3) and it follows from the same argument above using
[
BKV
, Proposition 5.3.9]
.

∎

Proposition 8.11
.

(1) The quotient
LK^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
is an ind-stack ind-locally of finite type
(2) the natural projection map

f:LK^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\to K({\mathcal{K}})^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}

is strongly pro-smooth.

In particular, both stacks
LK^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
and

K({\mathcal{K}})^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
admit gluing of sheaves.

Proof.

Since the stack of quasi maps
QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
is
an ind-stack ind-locally of finite type,
the uniformization isomorphism
LK^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\simeq QM^{(n)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)
in (
7.2
) implies
part (1).
On the other hand, the pullback of
f
along
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n}\to K({\mathcal{K}})^{(n)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}
is isomorphic to the natural projection map

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}}K({\mathcal{K}})^{(n)}/LK^{(n)}\longrightarrow{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(n)}.

Note that the
quotient
K({\mathcal{K}})^{(n)}/LK^{(n)}
is the

K({\mathcal{O}})^{(n)}
-torsor over
({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}\times\operatorname{Bun}_{K}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})

classifying a
K
-bundle
\mathcal{E}_{K}\in\operatorname{Bun}_{K}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
, a point
\underline{z}\in({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{n}
,
and a trivialization of
\mathcal{E}_{K}
on the formal neighborhood of the points
\underline{z}
in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
.
Since
K({\mathcal{O}})^{(n)}
is a projective limit of smooth schemes with smooth affine transition maps
(see, e.g.,
[
R
, Lemma 2.5.1]
)) and

\operatorname{Bun}_{K}({\mathbb{P}}^{1})
is a smooth stack locally of finite type
it implies that

f
is strongly pro-smooth.
The proposition follows.

∎

8.3.
Real and relative Satake categories

The loop group
K({\mathcal{K}})
, arc group
G({\mathcal{O}})
, and real arc group
G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})
) act natural on
{\operatorname{Gr}}
,
X({\mathcal{K}})
, and
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}

and we can form the quotient stacks
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
and
X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})

and the quotient semi-analytic stack
G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
(see Appendix
A.2
).
Let
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
,
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
, and
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
) be the dg-categories of
{\mathbb{C}}
-constructible complexes
on
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
,
X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})
, and
G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively (see Appnedix
A.3
).

By Proposition
8.4
,
there is an isomorphism of stacks

K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\simeq X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})

and hence a canonical equivalence

(8.4)

D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

We will call
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
the relative Satake category
and
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
the real Satake category.

8.4.
Perverse t-structures

Let
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

be the dg category of constructible
{\mathbb{C}}
-complexes on
G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}

and let

D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

to be the full subcategory
of
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
of complexes constructible with
respect to the
G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})
-orbits stratification

{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}=\{S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}
.
We have a natural equivalence

(8.5)

D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\simeq D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

Since
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is connected,
it follows from
[
N2
, Lemma 3.9.1]
that
the dimension

\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}=2\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle

of the
real spherical orbits
S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

are even numbers. Thus there is a classical perverse
t
-structure

(^{p}D^{\leq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}),{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}))

on

D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\simeq D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

given by the middle perversity function

p_{\mathbb{R}}:{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}
,
p_{\mathbb{R}}(S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})=-\frac{\dim_{\mathbb{R}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}}{2}=-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle
.
We will write

\operatorname{Perv}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})={{}^{p_{cl}}}D^{\leq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\cap{{}^{p_{cl}}}D^{\geq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

for the heart of the perverse
t
-structure.

We shall introduce a
t
-structure on
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

following
[
BKV
, Section 6]
.
For any finite type stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
we denote by

({{}^{p_{cl}}}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}),{{}^{p_{cl}}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}))

the classical perverse
t
-structure on
D({\mathcal{Y}})
.
According to
[
BKV
, Section 6.3.2]
, for every placid stack
{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq Y/H
there is a unique

!
-adapted
t
-structure on
(^{p}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}),{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}))

on

D({\mathcal{Y}})
characterized by

(8.6)

^{p}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}{{}^{p_{cl}}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})[\dim{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}]

where

{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{i\in I}{\mathcal{Y}}_{j}=Y_{j}/H

Following
[
BKV
, Section 2.4.8]
,
we say that an ind-stack

{\mathcal{Y}}
is placid stratified if there is a
stratification
\{{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I}
of
{\mathcal{Y}}
such that
(1)
each stratum
{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
is a placid stack
and the inclusion

j^{\alpha}:{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is a fp-locally closed embedding
(2) there is a presentation

{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{colim}_{j\in J}{\mathcal{Y}}^{j}

of
{\mathcal{Y}}
as a filtered colimit of closed substacks such that
each
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a finite union of the strata
{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}
.

Recall the following construction of

t
-structures functor
for placid stratified stack, admitting gluing of sheaves
in
[
BKV
, Proposition 6.4.2]
.

Proposition 8.12
.

Let
({\mathcal{Y}},\{{\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in I})

be a placid stratified stack, admitting gluing of sheaves, and equipped with
a perversity
p:I\to{\mathbb{Z}}
.
There is a unique
t
-structure
({{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}),{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}))

on
D({\mathcal{Y}})
satisfying

(8.7)

^{p}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}})=\{{\mathcal{F}}\in D({\mathcal{Y}})|(j^{\alpha})^{!}{\mathcal{F}}\in{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{S}}^{\alpha})[p(\alpha)].\}

The heart of the
t
-structure is denoted by

\operatorname{Perv}^{p}({\mathcal{Y}})={{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}})\cap{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}).

Note that every ind-placid stack
{\mathcal{Y}}

is a placid stratified stack and

[
BKV
, Lemma 5.5.6]
implies that it
admits glueing of sheaves.
Thus Proposition
8.3
, Corollary
8.4
and Lemma
8.7
imply
that
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\simeq X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})
together with the orbits stratification

\{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}})\simeq K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}^{\lambda}_{K}\}
is a placid stratified stack,
admitting glueing of sheaves.
We are mainly interested in the following perversity function

(8.8)

p_{X}:\Lambda_{A}^{+}\to{\mathbb{Z}},\ \ p_{X}(\lambda)=-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle

and we will call the unique
t
-structure

({{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})),{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})))

on
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

and
({{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}),{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}))

on
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

in Proposition
8.12
,
the

perverse
t
-structure
.
We will write

\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))={{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))\cap{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

\operatorname{Perv}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})={{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\cap{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

for the heart of the
t
-structure.

Denote by

j^{\lambda}_{X}:X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}})\to X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}})

the natural inclusion.
Let
\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}}))
be the category of perverse sheaves
on the placid stack
X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}})
.
Then for any
{\mathcal{L}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}}))
we have the

{\operatorname{IC}}
-complex

{\operatorname{IC}}({\mathcal{L}}):=\operatorname{Im}({{}^{p}}H^{0}(j^{\lambda}_{X,!}\mathcal{L}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle])\to{{}^{p}}H^{0}(j^{\lambda}_{X,*}\mathcal{L}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle]))\in\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

Note that
\omega_{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}})}\in\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}}))
, we will write

{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda}_{X}={\operatorname{IC}}(\omega_{X({\mathcal{K}})^{\lambda}/G({\mathcal{O}})})

Similarly, denote by

j^{\lambda}_{K}:K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
the natural inclusion.
Then for any
\mathcal{L}\in\operatorname{Perv}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K})
, we have
the
{\operatorname{IC}}
-complex

{\operatorname{IC}}({\mathcal{L}}):=\operatorname{Im}({{}^{p}}H^{0}(j^{\lambda}_{K,!}\mathcal{L}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle])\to{{}^{p}}H^{0}(j^{\lambda}_{K,*}\mathcal{L}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle]))\in\operatorname{Perv}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

and we will write

{\operatorname{IC}}_{K}^{\lambda}={\operatorname{IC}}(\omega_{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}}).

8.5.
Real-symmetric equivalence for affine Grassmannians

Consider the real analytic ind-scheme

\Omega K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
Introducing the stratification
{\mathcal{S}}_{K}=\{\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\}_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}
with strata
the

\Omega K_{c}
-quotients of
K({\mathcal{K}})
-orbits.
Let
D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

be the dg category of
K_{c}
-equivariant
{\mathbb{C}}
-constructible on
\Omega K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}

and
we set
D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{K}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
to be the full subcategory
of
D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

of complexes constructible with
respect to the stratification
{\mathcal{S}}_{K}
.

Consider the natural map

q:LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.

Lemma 8.13
.

The pullback functor

q^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

is fully-faithful and
induces
an equivalence

D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{K}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}).

Proof.

Consider the quotient
K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}

of
K({\mathcal{K}})
by the subgroup
LK_{c}
and the
natural embedding

{\operatorname{Gr}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}

sending
\gamma
to
(\gamma,eLK_{c})
.
It induces
an isomorphism of stacks

LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\simeq K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c})

(where
K({\mathcal{K}})
acts diagonally on
{\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}
)
and we have

q:LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\simeq K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}}}{{\to}}K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}

where
{\operatorname{pr}}
is induced by the natural projection map
{\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
Thus it suffices to show

{\operatorname{pr}}^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}))

is fully-faithful.
Note that we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong\ \ }
\scriptstyle{{\operatorname{pr}}^{!}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{lim}_{[n]}D(K({\mathcal{K}})^{n}\times{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{lim}_{[n]}({\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]})^{!}}
\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong\ \ \ \ \ }
\textstyle{\operatorname{lim}_{[n]}D(K({\mathcal{K}})^{n}\times({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}))}

where the horizontal equivalences come from the

\check{\operatorname{C}}
ech complexes for the coverings

{\operatorname{Gr}}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
and
{\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c})
, and the right vertical arrow are induced by the pull-back functors along the projections

{\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]}={\operatorname{id}}_{K({\mathcal{K}})^{n}}\times{\operatorname{pr}}:K({\mathcal{K}})^{n}\times({\operatorname{Gr}}\times K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c})\to K({\mathcal{K}})^{n}\times{\operatorname{Gr}}.

Since the space
K({\mathcal{K}})/LK_{c}\simeq K({\mathcal{O}})/K_{c}\simeq K({\mathcal{O}})_{+}\times K/K_{c}

is contractible (here
K({\mathcal{O}})_{+}\subset K({\mathcal{O}})
is the first congruence subgroup) the functor
({\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]})^{!}
is fully-faithful and it follows that

{\operatorname{pr}}^{!}
is fully-faithful.

We show that the resulting functor

q^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{K}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

is essentially surjective.
Since
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
admits gluing of sheaves,
Lemma
8.6

implies that
the category

D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
is generated by

j^{\lambda}_{K,*}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})
,
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
,
where
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\in D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})
.
Consider the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\lambda}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}^{\lambda}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j^{\lambda}_{K}}
\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}

The desired claim follows from the facts that
(1)

D_{{\mathcal{S}}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
is generated by

(i^{\lambda}_{+})_{*}(q_{\lambda}^{!})(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})
under colimit
and (2) the base change isomorphism for fp-locally closed embedding

q^{!}(j^{\lambda}_{K,*}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}))\simeq(i^{\lambda}_{+})_{*}(q^{\lambda})^{!}(\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})

(see, e.g.,
[
BKV
, Lemma 5.4.5]
).

∎

Theorem 8.14
.

There are natural equivalences

D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))\simeq D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

which are
t
-exact with respect to the perverse
t
-structures.

Proof.

Theorem
7.5
(the case when
m=1
) implies that
there is a
K_{c}
-equivariant stratified homeomorphism

\Omega K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}

which induces
a natural equivalence

(8.9)

D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{K}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}).

Now combining (
8.4
) and Lemma
8.13

we obtain the desired equivalences

D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle~\eqref{torsor-equ}}}{{\simeq}}D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{Lem}~\eqref{quasi-maps model equ}}}{{\simeq}}D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{K}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle~\eqref{trivialization equ}}}{{\simeq}}D_{{\mathcal{S}}_{\mathbb{R}}}(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\simeq

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{real model equ}}}{{\simeq}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}).

To check that the equivalences are
t
-exact it suffices to check that
it restricts to an equivalence

{}^{p}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
.
Note that the commutative diagram

\textstyle{K_{c}\backslash S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq LK_{c}\backslash{\mathcal{O}}^{\lambda}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\lambda}_{+}}
\scriptstyle{q^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}^{\lambda}_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{K}^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}

implies that there is a commutative square of functors

\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{q^{!}}
\scriptstyle{(j_{K}^{\lambda})^{!}}
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}}
\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(q^{\lambda})^{!}}
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}})}

In view of the characterization of

{}^{p}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})=\{\mathcal{F}\in D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})|(j_{K}^{\lambda})^{!}\mathcal{F}\in{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})[p_{X}(\lambda)]\}

in (
8.7
), we need to check
(q^{\lambda})^{!}
induces an equivalence

(q^{\lambda})^{!}:{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})[p_{X}(\lambda)]\simeq{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}).

Let
K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{i\in I}{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
be a placid presentation
with natural evaluation map
ev_{i}:K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
.
According to (
8.6
), we have

{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}{{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})[\dim{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}]

Since the fiber of the composition
ev_{i}\circ q^{\lambda}:K_{c}\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\to K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}

are contractible
and
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}/2=\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle=-p_{X}(\lambda)
, we have

(ev_{i}\circ q^{\lambda})^{!}:{{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})[\dim{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}]\to{{}^{p}}D^{\leq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})[\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}/2]\subset D^{\leq 0}(K_{c}\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})[\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}].

and the resulting map

(q^{\lambda})^{!}\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}(ev_{i}\circ q^{\lambda})^{!}:{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})[p_{X}(\lambda)]\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}({\mathcal{Y}}_{i})[\dim{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}-\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}/2]\to

\to D^{\leq 0}(K_{c}\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})[\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}/2]

factors through an equivalence

(q^{\lambda})^{!}:{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda})[p_{X}(\lambda)]\simeq{{}^{p}}D^{\geq 0}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}})\subset D^{\geq 0}(K_{c}\backslash S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda})[\dim_{\mathbb{R}}S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}/2]

The desired claim follows.
∎

9.
Affine Matsuki correspondence for sheaves

In this section we prove the
affine Matsuki correspondence for sheaves.

9.1.
The functor
\Upsilon

Consider the dg category
D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

of
{\mathbb{C}}
-constructible complexes on the semi-analytic stacks
LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
Consider the following correspondence

\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}

Define

(9.1)

\Upsilon=u_{!}q^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

Theorem 9.1

(Affine Matsuki correspondence for sheaves)
.

The functor
\Upsilon
defines an equivalence of categories

\Upsilon:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}).

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
9.1
.

9.2.
Bijection between local systems

Write
[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]=LK_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}
,
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]=LK_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,

[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]=LK_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}
, and

[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]=LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\in LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
Recall the Matsuki flow
\phi_{t}:{\operatorname{Gr}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}
in Theorem
3.9
.
As
\phi_{t}
is
LK_{c}
-equivariant, it descends to a flow

\tilde{\phi}_{t}:LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\to LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
and we define

\phi_{\pm}:LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\rightarrow\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}\ [\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\subset LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}},\ \ \gamma\rightarrow\underset{t\to\pm\infty}{\lim}\tilde{\phi}_{t}(\gamma).

Consider the following Cartesian diagrams:

\textstyle{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{+}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\scriptstyle{\phi_{+}^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{+}}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{+}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\textstyle{\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]}

\textstyle{[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{-}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\scriptstyle{\phi_{-}^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{-}}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{+}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\textstyle{\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]}

\textstyle{[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{-}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\scriptstyle{u^{\lambda}}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{-}^{\lambda}\ \ \ }
\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}

Here

i^{\lambda}_{\pm}
and
j^{\lambda}_{\pm}
are the natural
embeddings and
\phi^{\lambda}_{\pm}
(resp.
u^{\lambda}
) is the restriction of

\phi_{\pm}
(resp.
u
) along
j^{\lambda}_{+}
(resp.
j^{\lambda}_{-}
).

Lemma 9.2
.

We have the following:

(1)

There is a bijection between isomorphism classes of
local systems
\tau^{+}
on
[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]
,
local systems
\tau^{-}
on
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]
,
local systems
\tau
on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
, and local systems
\tau_{\mathbb{R}}
on
[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]=[LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]
, characterizing by the property that

\tau^{\pm}\simeq(\phi_{\pm}^{\lambda})^{*}\tau
and
\tau^{-}\simeq(u^{\lambda})^{*}\tau_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(2)

The map
u^{\lambda}
factors as

(9.2)

u^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{-}^{\lambda}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\lambda}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]

where
p^{\lambda}
is smooth of relative dimension
\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
. Moreover, we have

(p^{\lambda})^{*}\tau_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\tau
.

Proof.

Since the fibers of

\phi_{\pm}

are contractible, pull-back along
\phi^{\lambda}_{+}
(resp.
\phi^{\lambda}_{-}
) defines
an equivalence between

LK_{c}
-equivariant local systems on

\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}

and
LK_{c}
-equivariant local systems
on
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}
(resp.
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
).
We show that the fiber of
u^{\lambda}
is contractible, hence pull back along

u^{\lambda}
defines an equivalence between local systems on

[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]
and
LK_{c}
-equivariant local systems on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
.
Pick
y\in\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}
and let

LK_{c}(y)
,
LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)
be the stabilizers of

y
in
LK_{c}
and
LG_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.
The group
LK_{c}(y)
acts on the fiber

l_{y}:=(\phi_{-}^{\lambda})^{-1}(y)
and we have

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\simeq LK_{c}\times^{LK_{c}(y)}l_{y}
.
Moreover, under the isomorphism

[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\simeq LK_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\simeq LK_{c}(y)\backslash l_{y}
,
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\simeq LK_{c}(y)\backslash y
, and

[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]\simeq LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)\backslash y
,
the map
u^{\lambda}

takes the form

u^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\simeq LK_{c}(y)\backslash l_{y}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{-}^{\lambda}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\simeq LK_{c}(y)\backslash y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\lambda}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]\simeq LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)\backslash y,

where the first map is induced by the projection

l_{y}\rightarrow y
and
the second map is induced by the
inclusion
LK_{c}(y)\rightarrow LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)
.
We claim that the quotient
LK_{c}(y)\backslash LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)
is contractible,
hence

u^{\lambda}

has contractible fibers and
p^{\lambda}
is smooth of relative dimension
\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
. Part (1) and (2) follows.

Proof of the claim. Pick
y^{\prime}\in C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and let

K_{c}(y^{\prime})
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})
be the stabilizers of

y^{\prime}
in
K_{c}
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])
respectively.
The composition of the complex and real uniformizations of
\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))

LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{Prop.}\ref{uniformizations at complex x}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{Prop.}\ref{uniformizations at real x}}}{{\simeq}}G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}

identifies

LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)\backslash y\simeq[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]\simeq G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})\backslash y^{\prime}.

Hence we obtain a natural isomorphism

LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)\simeq{\operatorname{Aut}}([\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}])\simeq G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})

sending
LK_{c}(y)=K_{c}(y)\subset LG_{\mathbb{R}}(y)
to
K_{c}(y^{\prime})\subset G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})
.
Thus we reduce to show that the quotient
K_{c}(y^{\prime})\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})
is contractible.
This follows from the fact that evaluation map

K_{c}(y^{\prime})\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])(y^{\prime})\to K_{c}(y^{\prime})\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime}),\ \ \gamma(t^{-1})\to\gamma(0)

has contractible fibers and the quotient
K_{c}(y^{\prime})\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})
is contractible as

K_{c}(y^{\prime})
is a maximal compact subgroup of the Levi subgroup of
G_{\mathbb{R}}(y^{\prime})
.

∎

Recall the flow

\psi_{z}^{3}:QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}

in §
7.7
.
For
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
, we have the
critical manifold
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
, the stable manifold

S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
, and the unstable manifold
\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
.
We write

s_{\lambda}^{+}:S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,K,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}},\ \ \ \ \tilde{t}_{\lambda}:\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,K,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}

for the inclusion maps and we write

c_{\lambda}^{+}:S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda},\ \ \ \ \tilde{d}_{\lambda}:\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

for the contraction maps.
Note that all the
maps above are
K_{c}
-equivalent with respect to natural
K_{c}
-actions.
Recall that, by Lemma
7.7
, we have isomorphisms

\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}|_{i}\simeq\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,
\tilde{T}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}|_{0}\simeq T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,
for
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
and we write

s_{\lambda}^{-}:T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,K,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}},\ \ \ t_{\lambda}:\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},G,K,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}

for the restriction of
\tilde{t}_{\lambda}
and

c_{\lambda}^{-}:T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda},\ \ \ d_{\lambda}:\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

for the restriction of the contractions
\tilde{d}_{\lambda}
.

We write

k_{\lambda}:\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

for the restriction of
d_{\lambda}
and

p_{\lambda}:T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

for the natural quotient map.

Lemma 9.3
.

The map
k_{\lambda}:\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}\rightarrow C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
is a
K_{c}
-equivariant isomorphism.
There is a bijection between isomorphism classes of

K_{c}
-equivariant local systems
\omega^{+}
on
S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,

K_{c}
-equivariant local systems
\omega^{-}
on
T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,

K_{c}
-equivariant local systems
\omega
on
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,

K_{c}
-equivariant local systems
\tau
on
\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}
,
and local system
\omega_{\mathbb{R}}
on
G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,
characterizing by the property that

\omega^{\pm}\simeq(c_{\lambda}^{\pm})^{*}\omega
,
\tau\simeq(k_{\lambda})^{*}\omega

, and
(p_{\lambda})^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq(c_{\lambda}^{-})^{*}\omega

Proof.

The first claim follows from the fact that

\Omega K_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}\simeq C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\simeq K_{c}(\lambda)\backslash K_{c}
, where
K_{c}(\lambda)

is the stabilizer of
\lambda
in
K_{c}
, and the
K_{c}
-equivariant property of
k_{\lambda}
.
The second claim follows from the facts that the
contraction maps
c_{\lambda}^{\pm}
are

K_{c}
-equivariant and the
fibers of
c_{\lambda}^{\pm}
and the quotient

K_{c}\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])

are contractible.

∎

9.3.
Standard and co-standard sheaves

For each
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
and a local system

\tau
on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]

one has the standard sheaves

(9.3)

{\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\tau):=(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\text{\ \ and\ \ }{\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{-}(\lambda,\tau):=(j_{-}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau_{\mathbb{R}})

and co-standard sheaves

(9.4)

{\mathcal{S}}_{!}^{+}(\lambda,\tau):=(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{!}(\tau^{+})\text{\ \ and\ \ }{\mathcal{S}}_{!}^{-}(\lambda,\tau):=(j_{-}^{\lambda})_{!}(\tau_{\mathbb{R}}).

Here
\tau^{+}
and
\tau_{\mathbb{R}}
are local system
on
[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]
and
[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]

corresponding to
\tau

as in Lemma
9.2
.
Let
d_{\lambda}:=\operatorname{dim}\operatorname{Bun}_{G}(\mathbb{P}^{1})_{\mathbb{R}}-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]
.

Write

(9.5)

\iota_{+}^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\to[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mu}],\ \ \iota_{-}^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\to[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]

for the natural embeddings.
We recall the following fact, see
[
MUV
, Lemma 5.4]
.

Lemma 9.4
.

(1)

Consider
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{+}^{\mu}}}{{\to}}[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{+}^{\mu}}}{{\to}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]
.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\mu}])
. If
\mathcal{F}
is
smooth (= locally constant) on the trajectories of the flow
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
, then we have canonical isomorphisms

(\iota_{+}^{\mu})^{!}\mathcal{F}\simeq(\phi_{+}^{\mu})_{!}\mathcal{F}
and

(\iota_{+}^{\mu})^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq(\phi_{+}^{\mu})_{*}\mathcal{F}
.

(2)

Consider
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{-}^{\mu}}}{{\to}}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{-}^{\mu}}}{{\to}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]
where
\iota_{-}^{\mu}
is the natural embedding.
Let
\mathcal{F}\in D_{c}([\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}])
. If
\mathcal{F}
is
smooth (= locally constant) on the trajectories of the flow
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
and is supported on a finite dimensional substack
\mathscr{Y}\subset[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]
, then we have canonical isomorphisms

(\iota_{-}^{\mu})^{!}\mathcal{F}\simeq(\phi_{-}^{\mu})_{!}\mathcal{F}
and

(\iota_{-}^{\mu})^{*}\mathcal{F}\simeq(\phi_{-}^{\mu})_{*}\mathcal{F}
.

We shall show that the functor
\Upsilon
sends
standard sheaves to co-standard sheaves.
Introduce the following local system on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]

(9.6)

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}:=(\iota_{-}^{\lambda})^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}

where

(9.7)

(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mu})^{\vee}:=(i_{-}^{\mu})^{!}({\mathbb{C}})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]\ \text{\ and\ \ }\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}:=(\iota_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}[\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]

are local systems
on
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]
and
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
respectively and

\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}:=(p^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}[-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]+\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]
is the orientation sheaf for the smooth map
p^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\to[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]
in (
9.2
).

Lemma 9.5
.

For any local system

\tau
on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
we have

\Upsilon(S^{+}_{*}(\lambda,\tau))\simeq S^{-}_{!}(\lambda,\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})[d_{\lambda}].

Proof.

Let
\lambda,\mu\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
.
Consider the following diagram

(9.8)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 23.89659pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-23.89659pt\raise 47.89659pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
