131.43623pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 131.43623pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Let

\mathcal{G}=(j_{-}^{\mu})^{*}\Upsilon(S_{*}(\lambda,\tau))\simeq(j_{-}^{\mu})^{*}u_{!}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\simeq(u^{\mu})_{!}(i_{-}^{\mu})^{*}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})
.
It suffices to show that
\mathcal{G}\simeq 0
if
\lambda\neq\mu
and
\mathcal{G}\simeq(\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{R}}
if
\lambda=\mu
.

By Corollary
3.3
, the orbits
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}
and
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}
are trasversal to each
other, hence we have

(9.9)

(i_{-}^{\mu})^{*}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\simeq(i_{-}^{\mu})^{!}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mu}[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]].

where

(9.10)

(\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{\mu})^{\vee}=(i_{-}^{\mu})^{!}({\mathbb{C}})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]

is a local system on
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]
.
Thus

\mathcal{G}\simeq(u^{\mu})_{!}(i_{-}^{\mu})^{*}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{upper shriek}}}{{\simeq}}(u^{\mu})_{!}((i_{-}^{\mu})^{!}(i_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]\simeq

(u^{\mu})_{!}(s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]].

According to Lemma
9.2
the map
u^{\mu}
factors as

u^{\mu}:[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi_{-}^{\mu}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{\mu}}}{{\rightarrow}}[\mathcal{E}^{\mu}]

where
p^{\mu}
is smooth of relative dimension
\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\mu}]-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]
. Since
s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]\in D_{c}([\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}])

is smooth on the trajectories of the flow
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
, by Lemma
9.4
, we have

(9.11)

\mathcal{G}\simeq u^{\mu}_{!}(s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]\simeq p^{\mu}_{!}(\phi_{-}^{\mu})_{!}(s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{Lem}\ref{Braden}}}{{\simeq}}

\simeq p^{\mu}_{!}(\iota_{-}^{\mu})^{!}(s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]].

Here
\iota_{-}^{\mu}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]\rightarrow[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]
is the embedding.

If
\lambda\neq\mu
then
[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]\cap[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
is empty, thus we have

(\iota_{-}^{\mu})^{!}s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\mu}]]=0

and (
9.11
) implies
\mathcal{G}=0
.

If
\lambda=\mu
, then

[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]\cap[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]=[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
,

s=\iota_{-}^{\lambda}
,
\iota=\iota_{+}^{\lambda}
are closed embeddings
and by Lemma
9.2
we have

(u^{\lambda})_{!}(s)_{*}(\tau)\simeq(p^{\lambda})_{!}(\tau)\simeq\tau_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes(p^{\lambda})_{!}({\mathbb{C}})\simeq\tau_{\mathbb{R}}\otimes(\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee})_{\mathbb{R}}[\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\mu}]-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\mu}]],

\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\simeq\iota^{*}(\tau^{+})\otimes\iota^{!}{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\tau\otimes(\iota_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}\simeq\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime}_{\lambda}[-\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]

where
\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}
is the relative orientation sheaf on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
associated to
p^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\to[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]

and

(9.12)

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}:=(\iota_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}[\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]

is a local system on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
.
Now an elementary calculation shows that

\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{mG}}}{{\simeq}}(u^{\lambda})_{!}(s_{*}\iota^{!}(\tau^{+})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]]\simeq(u^{\lambda})_{!}(s_{*}(\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime})\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]\simeq

\simeq(u^{\lambda})_{!}(\iota_{-}^{\lambda})_{*}(\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\otimes(\iota_{-}^{\lambda})^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime})[\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]]\simeq(\tau\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})_{\mathbb{R}}[d_{\lambda}],

where

(9.13)

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}:=(\iota_{-}^{\lambda})^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}

is a local sytem on
[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]
and

d_{\lambda}=\operatorname{codim}[\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{codim}_{[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]+\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{E}^{\lambda}]-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]=\operatorname{dim}\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathbb{P}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))-\operatorname{dim}[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}].

The lemma follows.

∎

9.4.
Fully-faithfulness

We shall show that
\Upsilon
is fully-faithful.
Consider a diagram of closed substacks of
LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}

U_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{0}}}{{\longrightarrow}}U_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle j_{1}}}{{\rightarrow}}U_{2}\longrightarrow\cdot\cdot\cdot\rightarrow U_{k}\longrightarrow\cdot\cdot\cdot

such that

(1)

\bigcup_{i}U_{i}=LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
,

(2)

Each
U_{i}
is a finite union of
[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]
,

(3)

Each
j_{k}
is closed embedding.

Let
f_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
be the natural embedding and
we define

s_{i}=u\circ f_{i}:U_{i}\rightarrow LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}.

Note that each
s_{i}
is of finite type.

Lemma 9.6
.

For any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})}(\Upsilon(\mathcal{F}),\Upsilon(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})).

Proof.

Since the functor
u_{!}:D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\to}}D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
admits a continuous right adjoint
u^{!}
, it
sends compact objects to compact objects. It follows that
the functor

\Upsilon=:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q^{!}}}{{\simeq}}D_{{\mathcal{S}}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\subset D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u_{!}}}{{\to}}D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

sends compact objects to compact objects.
Thus
we can assume
both
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
are compact.
Then we can
choose
k
such that

\mathcal{F}=(j_{k})_{*}\mathcal{F}_{k}
and
(j_{k})_{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}
for

\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}\in D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})
.
We have

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})

and

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})}(\Upsilon(\mathcal{F}),\Upsilon(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}((s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime}).

We have to show that the map

(9.14)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})\rightarrow\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})

is an isomorphism.
Since
D_{c}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})
is generated by

w_{!}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+})
(resp.
w_{*}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+})
) for

[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]\subset U_{k}
(here
w_{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]\rightarrow U_{k}
is
natural inclusion), it suffices to verify
(
9.14
) for

\mathcal{F}_{k}=(w_{\lambda})_{!}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+})\text{\ \ and \ \ }\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k}\simeq(w_{\mu})_{*}(\tau_{\mu}^{+}).

Note that in this case the left hand side of (
9.14
) becomes

(9.15)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})=0\text{\ \ if \ \ }\lambda\neq\mu

(9.16)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{k})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda},\tau_{\lambda})\text{\ \ if \ \ }\lambda=\mu.

By Lemma
9.5
we have

(s_{k})_{!}((w_{\mu})_{*}(\tau_{\mu}^{+}))\simeq u_{!}(j_{k})_{*}(w_{\mu})_{*}(\tau_{\mu}^{+}))\simeq\Upsilon(S_{*}^{+}(\mu,\tau_{\mu}))\simeq(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}(\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}})[d_{\mu}],

where
\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}=\tau_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}
.
Therefore the right hand side of (
9.14
) becomes

(9.17)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}((w_{\lambda})_{!}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+}),(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}((w_{\mu})_{*}(\tau_{\mu}^{+}))\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]}(\tau^{+}_{\lambda},w_{\lambda}^{!}(s_{k})^{!}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}(\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}})[d_{\mu}])\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+},(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}(\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}})[d_{\mu}]).

Since
u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda}
and
j_{-}^{\mu}

are transversal,
we have

(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}\tau_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{*}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}\tau_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}[-d_{\lambda}]

where

(9.18)

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}=(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}[d_{\lambda}]

is a local system on
[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]

and, in view of the diagram (
9.8
), we get

(9.19)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{iso on hom,1}}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+},(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}[d_{\mu}])\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda}^{+},(u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{*}(j_{-}^{\mu})_{!}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}])}((\phi_{+}^{\lambda})^{*}\tau_{\lambda},\iota_{!}(u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])

\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}])}((\phi_{+}^{\lambda})^{*}\tau_{\lambda},\iota_{!}(u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L}[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])

\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda},(\phi_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\iota_{!}(u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L})[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}]).

Consider the case
\lambda\neq\mu
. Then
by Lemma
9.4
we have

(\phi_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\iota_{!}(u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L})[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])\simeq(\phi_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}\iota_{!}((u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}))[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])\simeq

\simeq(\iota^{\lambda}_{+})^{*}\iota_{!}((u^{\mu}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\mu,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}))[d_{\mu}-d_{\lambda}])=0,

here
\iota_{+}^{\lambda}:[\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}]\to[\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}]
,
and it follows from (
9.19
) that

(9.20)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})=0.

Hence we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\simeq}}\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\simeq 0\text{\ \ if\ \ }\lambda\neq\mu.

Consider the case
\lambda=\mu
. We have

(\phi_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\iota_{!}(u^{\lambda}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\lambda,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L})[d_{\lambda}-d_{\lambda}])\simeq(u^{\lambda}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\lambda,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}\simeq\tau_{\lambda}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\lambda}.

We claim that
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\lambda}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}
is the trivial local system
hence above isomorphism implies

(\phi_{+}^{\lambda})_{*}(\iota_{!}(u^{\lambda}\circ s)^{*}\tilde{\tau}_{\lambda,{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes\mathcal{L})[d_{\lambda}-d_{\lambda}])\simeq\tau_{\lambda},\ \text{\ if\ }\lambda=\mu,

and by (
9.19
), we obtain

(9.21)

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash U_{k})}(\mathcal{F}_{k},(s_{k})^{!}(s_{k})_{!}\mathcal{F}_{k}^{\prime})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda},\tau_{\lambda}).

By unwinding the definition of the map in (
9.14
), we obtain that (
9.14
) satisfies

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 54.0168pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-54.0168pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 189.17357pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.86674pt\raise-22.31972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 185.35947pt\raise-11.90321pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.34428pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\sim}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 175.35226pt\raise-22.31972pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.71112pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eqref{iso Hom, R}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 156.69675pt\raise-24.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 78.0168pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Hom}_{D([\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}])}(\tau_{\lambda},\tau_{\lambda})}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 261.17305pt\raise-32.49501pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

hence is an isomorphism.
The lemma follows.

To prove the claim, we observe that, up to cohomological shifts, we have

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{L_lambda}}}{{\simeq}}(s)^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}\simeq(p^{\lambda})^{*}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})^{\vee})\otimes\iota^{!}{\mathbb{C}}\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}[-]

\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{L}}}{{\simeq}}\iota^{*}((u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})[-].

Using the canonical isomorphisms
\iota^{!}(-)\simeq\iota^{*}(-)\otimes\iota^{!}{\mathbb{C}}
and

(p^{\lambda})^{!}(-)\simeq(p^{\lambda})^{*}(-)\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}[-]
, we see that

\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}\otimes\iota^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\prime}\simeq(p^{\lambda})^{*}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})^{\vee})\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}\otimes\iota^{!}((u\circ i_{+}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})[-]\simeq

\simeq(p^{\lambda})^{*}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})^{\vee})\otimes\operatorname{or}_{p^{\lambda}}^{\vee}\otimes(p^{\lambda})^{!}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}))[-]\simeq

\simeq(p^{\lambda})^{*}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}})^{\vee})\otimes(p^{\lambda})^{*}((j_{-}^{\lambda})^{!}{\mathbb{C}}))[-]\simeq{\mathbb{C}}[-].

The claim follows.
∎

9.5.
Proof of Theorem
9.1

Since the categories

D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

and

D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
are generated by
standard (resp. co-standard) objects,
Lemma
9.5
and Lemma
9.6
imply that the functor

\Upsilon:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\rightarrow D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

is essentially surjective and fully-faithful, and hence an equivalence.
This finishes the proof of Theorem
9.1
.

Remark 9.7
.

Let
D_{c}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\subset D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
be the
full subcategory consisting of constructible complexes that are extension by
zero off of a substack (equivalently, supported on a finite union
of
K({\mathcal{K}})
-orbits on
{\operatorname{Gr}}
).
We define
D_{!}(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

to the be full subcategory of

D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

consisting of all constructible complexes that are extensions by zero off of finite type substacks of

LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
The proof of Theorem
9.1
show that

\Upsilon
restricts to an equivalence

\Upsilon:D_{c}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D_{!}(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}).

10.
Nearby cycles functors and Radon transforms

We study the nearby cycles functors associated to the
quasi-maps in Section
7
and the Radon transform
for the real affine Grassmannian.

10.1.
A square of equivalences

Recall the quasi-map family

QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\to{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
in Section
7.2
.
Consider the base change

QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{\mathbb{R}}|_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}

along the natural inclusion
i{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
By
Proposition
6.6
and Proposition
7.1
,
we have the following cartesian diagram

Define the following nearby cycles functors

(10.1)

\Psi:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D_{{\mathcal{S}}}(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\rightarrow D(K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}),\ \ \mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Psi(\mathcal{F}):=i^{*}j_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}),

(10.2)

\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}:D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\rightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}),\ \ \mathcal{F}\rightarrow\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}(\mathcal{F})=(\bar{i})^{*}(\bar{j})_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}).

We also have the Radon transform

(10.3)

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

given by the restriction to
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\subset D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

of the
push-forward
p_{!}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

along the quotient map

p:G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
Denote by

D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})))

be the dg category of
{\mathbb{C}}
-constructible complexes
on
\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))
.

Here are the main results of this section.

Theorem 10.1
.

The nearby cycles functors and the Radon transform
induce equivalences of categories:

\Psi:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}),

\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}:D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}),

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}).

Theorem 10.2
.

We have a commutative square of equivalences

\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon}
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}\ \ }
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{D(\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}})))}

where the vertical equivalences in the lower triangle come from the
real and complex uniformization isomorphism

\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq\ \ }
\textstyle{\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \simeq}

The rest of the section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
10.1
and Theorem
10.2
.

10.2.
The nearby cycles functor
\Psi

For any
\lambda\in\mathcal{L}
and a
K_{c}
-equivaraint local system
\omega
on
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,
one has the standard and co-standard sheaves

{\mathcal{T}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\omega):=(s_{\lambda}^{+})_{*}(\omega^{+})\text{\ \ and\ \ \ }{\mathcal{T}}_{!}^{+}(\lambda,\omega):=(s_{\lambda}^{+})_{!}(\omega^{+})

in
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
(see Lemma
9.3
).
Recall the standard sheaf

{\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\tau)
in
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
(see (
9.3
)).

Proposition 10.3
.

The nearby cycles functor induces an equivalence

\Psi:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})
, which is the real-symmetric equivalence in Theorem
8.14
.
Moreover,
we have
\Psi({\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\tau))\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\omega)

Proof.

By Lemma
7.6
,
there is a
K_{c}
-equivariant
topological trivialization of the quasi-maps family

QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X,\infty)_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{C}}

and thus the nearby cycles functor is the same as the
real-symmetric equivalence in Theorem
8.14
.
The proposition follows.

∎

10.3.
The Radon transform

Recall the flow
\psi_{z}^{1}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}

in (
6.9
). By Lemma
6.3
, it
restricts to a
flow on the special fiber
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}|_{0}
with
critical manifolds
\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}}C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
and

S_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
, respectively
T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
, is the stable manifold, respectively unstable manifold, of
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
.
Let

t_{\lambda}^{-}:G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash T_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}\rightarrow G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
be the natural inclusion map.
According to Lemma
9.3
, for any
K_{c}
-equivariant local system

\omega
on
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
,
we have the standard and
co-standard sheaves

{\mathcal{T}}_{*}^{-}(\lambda,\omega):=(t_{\lambda}^{-})_{*}(\omega_{\mathbb{R}})\text{\ \ and\ \ \ }{\mathcal{T}}_{!}^{-}(\lambda,\omega):=(t_{\lambda}^{-})_{!}(\omega_{\mathbb{R}}).

Recall the Radon transform

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\longrightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

in (
10.3
).
The same argument as in the proof of Theorem
9.1
, replacing the
Matsuki flow
\phi_{t}:{\operatorname{Gr}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}
by the

{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
-flow
\psi_{z}^{1}:{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\to{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
and Lemma
9.2
by Lemma
9.3
,
gives us:

Proposition 10.4
.

The Radon transform defines an equivalence of categories

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}).

Moreover, for any
K_{c}
-equivariant local system
\omega

on
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
we have

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{T}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\omega))\simeq{\mathcal{T}}^{-}_{!}(\lambda,\omega\otimes\mathcal{L}_{\lambda})[d_{\lambda}].

Here we regard the local system
\mathcal{L}_{\lambda}
in (
9.13
) as
a local system on
C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}
via the isomorphism

k_{\lambda}:\Omega G_{c}\backslash\mathcal{O}_{c}^{\lambda}\simeq C_{\mathbb{R}}^{\lambda}

in Lemma
9.3
.

10.4.
The functor
\Psi_{\mathbb{R}}

Note that, by Proposition
6.6
, the map
LG_{\mathbb{R}}^{(\sigma_{2})}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{G_{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}({\mathbb{R}}))\times i\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}\rightarrow i\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}
is isomorphic to a constant family. It implies

Proposition 10.5
.

The nearby cycles functor

\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}:D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

is an equivalence satisfying

\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{S}}_{!}^{-}(\lambda,\tau))\simeq{\mathcal{T}}_{!}^{-}(\lambda,\omega)
.

10.5.
Proof of Theorem
10.1
and Theorem
10.2

We already proved Theorem
10.1
.
We have
\Psi(\mathcal{F})\simeq i^{*}j_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})\simeq i^{!}j_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}[1])

and the natural arrow

(f_{0})_{!}i^{!}\rightarrow(\bar{i})^{!}f_{!}
gives rise to a natural transformation

(10.4)

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\Psi(\mathcal{F})\simeq(f_{0})_{!}i^{!}j_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}[1])\rightarrow(\bar{i})^{!}f_{!}j_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}[1])\simeq(\bar{i})^{!}(\bar{j})_{!}(f^{0})_{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes{\mathbb{C}}_{i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}[1])\simeq

\simeq\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}\circ\Upsilon(\mathcal{F}).

Moreover, it follows from Lemma
9.5
, Proposition
10.3
,
Proposition
10.4
, and Proposition
10.5
that (
10.4
) is an isomorphism for
the standard sheaf
{\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\tau)
.
Since the category
D_{c}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
is generated by

{\mathcal{S}}_{*}^{+}(\lambda,\tau)
, it implies (
10.4
) is an isomorphism.
Theorem
10.2
follows.

11.
Compatibility of Hecke actions

Recall the derived Satake category
D(G(\mathcal{O})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
is naturally monoidal with respect to convolution.
We will write
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
for the convolution product of
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in D(G(\mathcal{O})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
.

Here we enhance the equivalences and commutative square of Theorems
10.1
and
10.2
to
D(G(\mathcal{O})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
-modules.
Roughly speaking, we will take advantage of the natural right actions on the categories involved, whereas the prior Radon transforms were performed on the left.

11.1.
Hecke actions

First, the affine Matsuki correspondence for sheaves

\Upsilon:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

is naturally an equivalence of
D(G(\mathcal{O})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
-modules by convolution on the right. To see this,
recall
\Upsilon
is the restriction to
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\subset D(LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
of the push-forward

u_{!}:D(LK_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

along the quotient map
u:LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\rightarrow LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}
.
We can equip this construction with compatibility with convolution on the right by using the commutative action diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces LK_{c}\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O})}{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u\times{\operatorname{id}}}
\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O})}{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\textstyle{LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}}

and its natural iterations.

Similarly,
the Radon equivalence

\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

is naturally an equivalence of
D_{c}(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
-modules by convolution on the right.
To see this,
recall
\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}
is
the restriction to
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\subset D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

of the
push-forward
p_{!}:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\rightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

along the quotient map

p:G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\to G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
We can equip this construction with compatibility with convolution on the right by using the commutative action diagram

\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash G({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p\times{\operatorname{id}}}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash G({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathbb{R}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}}

and its natural iterations.

11.2.
From complex to real kernels

Following
[
N1
]
, nearby cycles in the real Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}

over
i{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

gives a functor

(11.1)

\psi:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\longrightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

Namely,
there is a canonical diagram of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-equivariant maps

(11.2)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Gr}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{0}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{i}

where we view
G_{\mathbb{R}}\subset LG^{(2)}_{\mathbb{R}}
as the constant group-scheme.
One defines
\psi=i^{*}j_{*}\pi^{*}f_{\mathbb{R}}
where we write
f_{\mathbb{R}}:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(G_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})

for the forgetful functor.

Note the domain and codomain of
\psi
both have natural convolution monoidal structures.
To equip
\psi
with a monoidal structure, we proceed as follows.

Let
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}
be the moduli of
x_{1},x_{2}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

G
-torsors on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
\phi
a trivialization of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{x_{1},x_{2}\}
, and
\alpha
an isomorphism from
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{x_{1},x_{2}\}
.
Let
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}
be the real form of
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}
with respect to the twisted conjugation that exchanges
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.

Then
there is a canonical diagram of
G_{\mathbb{R}}
-equivariant maps

(11.3)

\textstyle{G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{j}

\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}|_{0}\simeq G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times^{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{i}

Moreover, the convolution maps on the end terms naturally extend to the entire diagram. By standard identities, we arrive at a canonical isomorphism
\psi({\mathcal{F}}_{1}\star{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq\psi({\mathcal{F}}_{1})\star\psi({\mathcal{F}}_{2})
. By using iterated versions of the above moduli spaces, we may likewise equip
\psi
with the associativity constraints of a monoidal structure.

11.3.
Compatibility of actions

Note we can view the Radon equivalence
\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}
as an equivalence of
D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
-modules
via the monoidal functor

\psi:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\longrightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

Now we have the following further compatibility of our constructions.

Theorem 11.1
.

Via the monoidal functor

\psi:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\longrightarrow D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

the equivalences

\Psi:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}),

\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}:D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

of Theorem
10.1
and commutative square

\textstyle{D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon}
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Upsilon_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{D(LG_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Psi_{{\mathbb{R}}}\ \ }
\textstyle{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}).}

of Theorem
10.2
are naturally of
D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
-modules.

Proof.

We will focus on the compatibility for the top row and indicate the moduli spaces needed. We leave it to the reader to pass to sheaves and apply standard identities. The compatibility for the bottom row and entire square can be argued similarly.

Let
QM^{(2)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}
be the moduli of
x_{1},x_{2}\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
{\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}

G
-torsors on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
,
\sigma
a section of
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\times^{G}X
over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{x_{1},x_{2}\}
, and
\alpha
an isomorphism from
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
to
{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
over
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{x_{1},x_{2}\}
.
Let
QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}
be the real form of
QM^{(2)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}
with respect to the twisted conjugation that exchanges
x_{1}
and
x_{2}
.

Then
there is a canonical diagram of
K_{c}
-equivariant maps

(11.4)

\textstyle{LK_{c}\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O})}{\operatorname{Gr}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces LK_{c}\backslash G({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G(\mathcal{O})}{\operatorname{Gr}}\times i{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\simeq QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{>0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{j}

\textstyle{QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{i\mathbb{R}_{\geq 0}}}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces QM^{(\sigma_{2})}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},X)_{{\mathbb{R}}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(\sigma_{2})}_{{\mathbb{R}}}|_{0}\simeq K_{c}\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times^{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}}
\scriptstyle{i}

Note we could equivalently obtain diagram (
11.4
) by taking diagram (
11.3
)
and quotienting by the left action of the group-scheme
LK^{(\sigma_{2})}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

As with the convolution maps in diagram (
11.3
), the actions maps on the end terms of diagram
(
11.4
) naturally extend to the entire diagram. By standard identities, we arrive at a canonical isomorphism
\Psi({\mathcal{M}}\star{\mathcal{F}})\simeq\Psi({\mathcal{M}})\star\psi({\mathcal{F}})
. By using iterated versions of the above moduli spaces, we may likewise equip
\Psi
with the associativity constraints of a module map.
∎

12.
Compatibility with fusion product

We show that the real-symmetric equivalences
in Theorem
8.14

are compatible with the natural fusion products

12.1.
Fusion product for
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}

We first define fusion product for the real Satake category.
Consider the
family

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}

obtained by the restriction of the family

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(2)}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

along
embedding

{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
sending
t\to(t,-t)
.
The natural action of
G({\mathcal{O}})^{(2)}_{\mathbb{R}}
on
{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}^{(2)}

is compatible with the factorization isomorphisms in Section
4.3
and it follows that
there are
Cartesian diagrams

\textstyle{(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \ \ \ \ j_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{(G({\mathcal{O}})^{(2)}_{\mathbb{R}}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}_{\mathbb{R}})|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}
\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

For any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
, we define the fusion product
\mathcal{F}\star_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}

as the following nearby cycles:

\mathcal{F}\star_{f}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:=(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})

where
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}
is the constant sheaf on
{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}
.
Like in the case of complex reductive groups,
there is a natural monoidal structure on

D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

given by the convolution product:
consider the convolution diagram

(12.1)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\times{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\leftarrow}}G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle q}}{{\rightarrow}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}:=G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times^{G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m}}{{\to}}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}}

where
p
and
q
are the natural quotient maps and

m(x,y\mod G_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}}))=xy\mod G_{{\mathbb{R}}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})
.
For any
\mathcal{F}_{1},\mathcal{F}_{2}\in D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})
,
the convolution
is defined as

\mathcal{F}_{1}\star\mathcal{F}_{2}=m_{!}(\mathcal{F}_{1}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{F}_{2})

where
\mathcal{F}_{1}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{F}_{2}\in D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})
is the unique complex
such that
q^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{1}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{F}_{2})\simeq p^{*}(\mathcal{F}_{1}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}_{2})
.

Proposition 12.1
.

(1) There is a natural isomorphism

\star_{f}\simeq\star:D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\to D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

of functors.
(2) The fusion and convolution product are
t
-exact with respect to the
perverse
t
-structure.

Proof.

Parts (1) follows from the same proof as in the complex case using the real global convolution diagram
in
[
N2
, Section 6.3]
.
Part (2) follows from part (1) and the fact that
the real convolution map
m
is a stratified semismall map, see
[
N2
, Section 3.8]
.

∎

12.2.
Fusion product
for
X({\mathcal{K}})

We define and study the fusion product for the relative Satake category.

Consider the base change

{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{C}}

of
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\to({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}
along the embedding

{\mathbb{C}}\hookrightarrow({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2},\ \ t\to(t,-t).

Since the action of the ind-group scheme

\textstyle{(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\simeq(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where

Since

For any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))

as the following nearby cycles

\mathcal{F}\star_{f}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:=i^{*}j_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})

here we view
\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}

.

Note that the left adjoint

.
Indeed, we have

\operatorname{Hom}(i^{*}s_{*}\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}(s_{*}\mathcal{F},i_{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}(s_{*}\mathcal{F},s_{*}(i_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}(\mathcal{F},(i_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal{M},s^{!}j_{*}(s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}(s_{*}\mathcal{M},j_{*}(s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}(j^{!}s_{*}\mathcal{M},(s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq

\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}((s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})^{!}\mathcal{M},(s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}\mathcal{M}^{\prime})\simeq{\operatorname{Hom}}((j_{\mathbb{R}})^{!}\mathcal{M},\mathcal{M}^{\prime})

where the last isomorphisms follow from the
fact that the functors
s_{*}
and
(s|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})_{*}
are fully-faithful.
There is an isomorphism

\mathcal{F}\star_{f}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\simeq(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})

Theorem 12.2
.

(1)

The equivalences

D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))\simeq D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{{\mathbb{R}}})

are compatible with the fusion products

(2)

The fusion product for

D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
(resp.
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
)

t
-exact with respect to the perverse
t
-structure.

Proof.

Proof of (1). The compatibility for
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))\simeq D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
follows from the definition.
Consider the family of
embeddings

\iota_{a}:{\mathbb{C}}\to({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}\ \ \ t\to(t+ai,-t+ai)

parametrized by
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
There is a natural isomorphism

(12.2)

(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{\iota_{a}({\mathbb{C}})}\simeq(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{\iota_{0}({\mathbb{C}})}=(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{C}}}

induced by the translation isomorphism

and
the stratified homeomorphism in Theorem

restricts to a stratified homeomorphism

(12.3)

{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\simeq(\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{\iota_{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0})}

Let us consider the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\ \ j_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(

)
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{(\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\scriptstyle{h^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}}

\textstyle{\Omega K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \ \ \ i_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{\mathbb{R}}}
\textstyle{(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where

h^{(2)}:(\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{\iota_{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\operatorname{pr}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{\iota_{1}({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eqref{translation}}}{{\simeq}}(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}}

and

{\operatorname{pr}}:(\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})\to(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})

is
the natural map
induced by the natural inclusion
\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\to K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}
.
Note that there is an isomorphism

(\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})\simeq K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}/\Omega K_{c}^{(2)})

where
K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}
-acts diagonally, and
the functor

{\operatorname{pr}}^{!}\simeq(h^{(2)})^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}})\simeq\operatorname{colim}D(({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})^{[n]}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}})\longrightarrow D((\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}})\simeq

\simeq\operatorname{colim}D(({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}/\Omega K_{c}^{(2)})^{[n]})

is induced by the pullback functors
({\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]})^{!}
along the
projection maps between
the terms of the
\check{\operatorname{C}}
ech complexes:

{\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]}:({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}/\Omega K_{c}^{(2)})^{[n]}=({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}/\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}))\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)})^{n}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle}}{{\rightarrow}}

\rightarrow({\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})^{[n]}\simeq{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)})^{n}

Note that
the Gram-Schmidt factorization in Proposition
5.4

implies that quotient

K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}/\Omega K_{c}^{(2)}\simeq K({\mathcal{O}})^{(2)}

is strongly pro-smooth, that is,
K({\mathcal{O}})^{(2)}
is a projective limit of smooth schemes with smooth affine transition maps.
Thus the map
{\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]}
is also strongly pro-smooth and it follows from
[
BKV
, Proposition 5.2.7 (d) and Lemma 5.4.5]

that
({\operatorname{pr}}^{[n]})^{!}
and hence
{\operatorname{pr}}^{!}\simeq(h^{(2)})^{!}
commutes with nearby cycles, that is,
we have

h^{!}(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(-)\simeq(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(h^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}})^{!}(-).

Since
h^{!}:D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(\Omega K_{c}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
gives rise to the equivalence

D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\simeq D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

in Theorem
8.14
,
for any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
, there is a natural isomorphism

h^{!}(\mathcal{F}\star_{f}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq h^{!}((i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}))\simeq(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(h^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{R}}^{\times}})^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})\simeq

\simeq(i_{\mathbb{R}})^{*}(j_{\mathbb{R}})_{*}(h^{!}\mathcal{F}\boxtimes h^{!}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})\simeq h^{!}\mathcal{F}\star_{f}h^{!}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}.

Part (1) follows.

Since the equivalences in (1) are
t
-exact,
Part (2) follows from part (1) and Lemma
12.1
.

∎

13.
Applications

We provide numerous applications of the main results to real and relative
Langlands duality.

13.1.
t
-exactness criterion and semi-simplicity of Hecke actions

Theorem 13.1
.

The Hecke actions on
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
and
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
(resp.

D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
)
are
t
-exact if and only if
X
is quasi-split (resp.
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split).

Proof.

It was shown in
[
N2
, Theorem 1.2.3]
that
the nearby cycles functor

\psi:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

is
t
-exact if and only if
G_{\mathbb{R}}
is quasi-split.
Since the
Hecke action on the real Satake category
is given by the convolution
\mathcal{F}\star\psi(\mathcal{F})
,
the
t
-exactness for the convolution in Lemma
12.1

implies the desired claim for the case
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
.
We deduce the case of
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
from
Theorem
8.14

and Theorem
11.1
.

∎

Remark 13.2
.

It would be nice is one can find a
direct geometric proof for the
t
-exactness criterion
for the relative Satake category.

Theorem 13.3
.

The Hecke action on the real and relative Satake categories
satisfies the conclusion of decomposition theorem. That is,
for any semi-simple complexes
\mathcal{M}\in D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
and

\mathcal{F}\in D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
or
D(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
or

D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
, the convolutions

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{M}
is again semi-simple.

In particular,
the nearby cycles functor

\psi:D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})\to D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

preserves semi-simplicity.

Proof.

By the real-symmetric equivalence Theorem
8.14
and Theorem
11.1
,
it suffices to prove the case of relative Satake categories.
Pick a placid presentation
X({\mathcal{K}})=\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}\operatorname{lim}_{j\in J}Y^{i}_{j}
.
We can assume
the twisted product
\mathcal{F}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{M}\in D(X({\mathcal{K}})\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}{\operatorname{Gr}})

is supported on
Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}/G({\mathcal{O}})

for some
i\in I
and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{T}^{+}
. One can find
an (large enough) index
i^{\prime}\in I
such that the image of the action map

a:Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}\to X({\mathcal{K}})

lands in
Y^{i^{\prime}}
. Note that
Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}
is
G({\mathcal{O}})
-placid and
the induced map

a:Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}\to Y^{i^{\prime}}.

is a proper morphism.
Thus by
[
EGA IV
, Theorem 8.10.5]
, one can find
a placid presentation
Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}\simeq\operatorname{lim}_{j\in J}Z_{j}
, an index
j\in J
,
and a proper morphism
\bar{a}:Z_{j}\to Y_{j}^{i^{\prime}}

such that there is a Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Y^{i}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}\overline{G({\mathcal{O}})t^{\lambda}G({\mathcal{O}})}/G({\mathcal{O}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ a}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{Y^{i^{\prime}}/G({\mathcal{O}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{Z_{j}/G({\mathcal{O}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \bar{a}}
\textstyle{Y^{i^{\prime}}_{j}/G({\mathcal{O}})}

Moreover, we can assume

\mathcal{F}\tilde{\boxtimes}\mathcal{M}\simeq h^{!}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
for some semisimple object

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\in D(Z_{j}/G({\mathcal{O}}))
.
Since
\bar{a}
is proper,
the Decomposition Theorem implies
\bar{a}_{*}(\mathcal{F})
is semi-simple and
the proper base change theorem implies that

\mathcal{F}\star\mathcal{M}\simeq a_{*}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{M})\simeq a_{*}h^{!}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq p^{!}(\bar{a})_{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\prime})

is semi-simple.

∎

13.2.
Formality and commutativity of dg Ext algebras

We have the monoidal abelian Satake equivalence

\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\simeq\operatorname{Perv}(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G}):\ \ \ V\to\ \operatorname{IC}_{V}.

By restricting the Hecke action to the subcagegory
\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\simeq\operatorname{Perv}(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
, we obtain
a monoidal action of
\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
on
D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
and
D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
.
Let
\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}})/G({\mathcal{O}})}\in\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
be the
dualizing complex on
the closed
G({\mathcal{O}})
-orbit

X({\mathcal{K}})^{0}\simeq X({\mathcal{O}})
and
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})

be the
{\operatorname{IC}}
-complex of the closed
G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})
-orbit
S^{0}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Let
{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}:=\operatorname{IC}_{{\mathcal{O}}(G^{\vee})}

(an ind-object in
\operatorname{Perv}(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{G})
)
be the image of the regular representation

{\mathcal{O}}(G^{\vee})

under the abelian Satake equivalence.
Since
{\mathcal{O}}(G^{\vee})
is a ring object in
\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
,

{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}
is naturally a ring object
in
D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
, that is, there is a natural
homomorphism

(13.1)

m:{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\to{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}

satisfying the unit and associativity properties. It follows that the
RHom spaces

A_{X}:=R{\operatorname{Hom}}_{D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))}(\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}})/G({\mathcal{O}})},\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}}))/G({\mathcal{O}})}\star\operatorname{IC}_{reg})

\ A_{\mathbb{R}}:=R{\operatorname{Hom}}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star\operatorname{IC}_{reg})

are naturally dg-algebras with natural
G^{\vee}
-actions, known as the the de-equivariantized Ext algebras for the symmetric and real Satake categories respectively.
We denote by
H^{\bullet}(A_{X})=\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))}(\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}})/G({\mathcal{O}})},\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}}))/G({\mathcal{O}})}\star\operatorname{IC}_{reg}))

and
H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})=\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star\operatorname{IC}_{reg})

the corresponding cohomology algebras with trivial differentials.

Theorem 13.4
.

(1) There is a
G^{\vee}
-equivariant isomorphism of
dg algebras

A_{X}\simeq A_{\mathbb{R}}
inducing a
G^{\vee}
-equivariant isomorphism
of algebras
H^{\bullet}(A_{X})\simeq H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})
.

(2) The dg algebra
A_{X}
(resp.
A_{\mathbb{R}}
) is
formal, that is, they are quasi-isomorphic to the
cohomology algebras

H^{\bullet}(A_{X})
(resp.
H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})
)
with trivial differential.

(3)
The algebra
H^{\bullet}(A_{X})
(resp.
H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})
) is commutative.

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Theorem
8.14

and Theorem
11.1
.

For part (2) and (3), using (1), it suffices to prove
that
A_{X}
is formal and
H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})
is commutative.
The formality of
A_{X}
is proved in
[
CY
]
using a pointwise purity result for

{\operatorname{IC}}
-complexes of
G({\mathcal{O}})
-obits in
X({\mathcal{K}})
.
The proof of the commutativity of
H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})

is similar to the case of complex groups.
Note that
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\simeq\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})

where
\psi
is the monoidal functor in (
11.1
).
Recall that
{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}
is a
commutative ring object
in
D(G({\mathcal{O}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
, that is,
there is a natural isomorphism

m\circ\sigma\simeq m:{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\to{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}

where

(13.2)

\sigma:{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\simeq{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}

is the commutativity constraint
of the convolution product.
The monoidal structure of
\psi

gives rise to a multiplication morphism of
\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})
:

m_{\mathbb{R}}:\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\simeq\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi(m)}}{{\longrightarrow}}\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})

satisfying the unit and associativity properties
and there is a natural isomorphism

(13.3)

m_{\mathbb{R}}\circ\sigma_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq m_{\mathbb{R}}:\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\to\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})

where

\sigma_{\mathbb{R}}:\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\simeq\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi(\sigma)}}{{\simeq}}\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\simeq\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}).

Note that
the multiplication of

H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}})=\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star\operatorname{IC}_{reg})\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))

is induced from the multiplication morphism

m_{\mathbb{R}}
of
\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})
:
for
x:\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\to\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[i]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))
,
y:\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\to\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[j]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{j}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))

there exists an unique map

x\tilde{\boxtimes}y:\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\boxtimes}\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\to\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[i]\tilde{\boxtimes}\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[j]\in\operatorname{Ext}^{i+j}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\tilde{\boxtimes}\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\tilde{\boxtimes}\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))

such that

q^{*}(x\tilde{\boxtimes}y)=p^{*}(x\boxtimes y)\in\operatorname{Ext}^{i+j}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{K}}_{\mathbb{R}})\times{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\boxtimes\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\boxtimes\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))

(where
p
and
q
are the maps in the convolution diagram (
12.1
))
and the product
x\star y\in\operatorname{Ext}^{i+j}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))
is given by

x\star y=m_{!}(x\tilde{\boxtimes}y):\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\to\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[i]\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[j]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m_{\mathbb{R}}}}{{\to}}\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})[i+j]

Since the commutativity constraint
\sigma

exchanges the factors of
{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}
, it follows that

\sigma_{\mathbb{R}}
exchanges the factors of

\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})\star\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg})
and the isomorphism (
13.3
)
implies that the algebra

\operatorname{Ext}^{\bullet}_{D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})}(\delta_{\mathbb{R}},\psi({\operatorname{IC}}_{reg}))

is commutative.

∎

Theorem
13.4
implies the following spectral descriptions of the
full subcategories

D_{c}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))_{0}\subset D(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
and
D_{c}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})_{0}\subset D(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
in Section
1.2.4
.
Consider the Hamiltonian duals
M^{\vee}_{X}=\operatorname{Spec}(H^{\bullet}(A_{X}))
and
M_{\mathbb{R}}^{\vee}=\operatorname{Spec}(H^{\bullet}(A_{\mathbb{R}}))

of
X
and
G_{\mathbb{R}}
.

Theorem 13.5
.

(1) There is a
G^{\vee}
-equivariant isomorphism

M^{\vee}_{X}\simeq M^{\vee}_{\mathbb{R}}
.
(2)There are equivalences of categories

(13.4)

D_{c}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Coh}(M^{\vee}_{X}/G^{\vee})\ \ \ \ D_{c}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Coh}(M_{\mathbb{R}}^{\vee}/G^{\vee}).

Proof.

Part (1) follows from Theorem
13.4
and part (2)
follows from the formality of dg Ext algebras
A_{X}
and
A_{\mathbb{R}}

and the general Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem, see the details in
[
CMNO
, Theorem 5.5]
.
∎

13.3.
Identification of dual groups

In this section we show that there is an isomorphism
between the dual
group
H_{real}^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}
of
G_{\mathbb{R}}

introduced in
[
N2
]
and the dual group
H_{sph}^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}

of
X
introduced in
[
GN1
]
.

13.3.1.
Construction of
H_{real}^{\vee}

Definition 13.6
.

Let
Q_{\mathbb{R}}\subset\operatorname{Perv}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
be
be the full subcategory whose objects are isomorphic to
direct sum of perverse sheaves that appears in the summand
of
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}
for some
V\in\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
.
Let
Q_{X}\subset\operatorname{Perv}(X({\mathcal{K}})/G({\mathcal{O}}))
be
be the full subcategory whose objects are isomorphic to
direct sum of perverse sheaves that appears in the summand
of
\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}})/G({\mathcal{O}})}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}
for some
V\in\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
.
Let
Q_{K}\subset\operatorname{Perv}(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})
be
be the full subcategory whose objects are isomorphic to
direct sum of perverse sheaves that appears in the summand
of
\omega_{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{0}}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}
for some
V\in\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
.

Consider the perverse Hecke actions

(13.5)

\star^{p}:\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\times Q\to Q\ \ \ \ \ {\operatorname{IC}}_{V}\star^{p}\mathcal{F}:=\bigoplus_{i\in{\mathbb{Z}}}{{}^{p}}H^{i}({\operatorname{IC}}_{V}\star\mathcal{F})

where
Q=Q_{\mathbb{R}}
,
Q_{X}
or
Q_{K}
.

Proposition 13.7
.

(1) The abelian category
Q_{\mathbb{R}}
is semi-simple
and irreducible objects are intersection cohomology sheaves on the
orbits closures of strata
S^{\lambda}_{\mathbb{R}}
,
\lambda\in\sigma(\Lambda_{T})
(see (
2.2
)), with
coefficients in trivial local systems.
There exists a unique associativity and commutativity constraints
for the
category
Q_{\mathbb{R}}
equipped with the convolution product
\star

such that
(Q_{\mathbb{R}},\star)
is a neutral Tannakian category with
fiber functor

\operatorname{H}^{*}:Q_{\mathbb{R}}\to\operatorname{Vect}
given by cohomology.
The Tannakian group of
Q_{\mathbb{R}}
is isomorphic to the
connected complex reductive subgroup
H_{real}^{\vee}\subset G^{\vee}
of the dual group associated to
G_{\mathbb{R}}
in
[
N2
]
and there is a horizontal tensor equivalence in the following diagram of tensor functors

\textstyle{\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q_{\mathbb{R}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Rep}(H_{real}^{\vee})}

where left vertical arrow is given by the perverse Hecke action on
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}
.

(2)
There exists unique associativity and commutativity constraints
for the
category
Q_{X}
(resp.
Q_{K}
) equipped with the fusion product
\star_{f}
such that
there are horizontal tensor equivalences
in the following commutative diagram of
tensor functors

(13.6)
