where vertical arrows are given by the perverse Heck-actions
\star^{p}
of
\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}
(
13.5
) on
\omega_{X({\mathcal{O}})/G({\mathcal{O}})}
,
\omega_{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{0}}
,
and
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}
respectively.

Proof.

The semi-simplicity of the Hecke action in
Theorem
13.3

implies that
the category
Q_{\mathbb{R}}
is the same as the full subcategory

Q({\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})\subset\operatorname{Perv}(G_{\mathbb{R}}({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbb{R}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}_{\mathbb{R}})
introduced
[
N2
]
whose objects are isomorphic to
subquotients of perverse sheaves that appears in the summand
of
\delta_{\mathbb{R}}\star{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}
for some
V\in\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
.
Now part (1) follows from the main results in
[
N2
]
.

Proposition
12.2

implies that there are equivalence

Q_{\mathbb{R}}\simeq Q_{X}\simeq Q_{K}
compatible with the
Hecke action, and the convolution product on
Q_{\mathbb{R}}

and the fusion products on
Q_{X}
and
Q_{K}
.
Thus we can
transport the
associativity and commutativity constraints of
(Q_{\mathbb{R}},\star)

through the above equivalence to

(Q_{X},\star_{f})
and
(Q_{K},\star_{f})
. Now the commutativity of (
13.6
)
follows again from part (1) and Theorem
11.1
.

∎

Corollary 13.8
.

There are tensor equivalences
Q_{X}\simeq Q_{K}\simeq\operatorname{Rep}(H_{real}^{\vee})
.
The abelian category
Q_{X}
(resp.
Q_{K}
) is semi-simple with irreducible objects

{\operatorname{IC}}_{X}^{\lambda}
(resp.
{\operatorname{IC}}_{K}^{\lambda}
),
\lambda\in\sigma(\Lambda_{T})
.

13.3.2.
Construction of
H_{sph}^{\vee}

Fix a pole point
0\in{\mathbb{P}}^{1}

and consider
the stack of quasi-maps
Z:=QM^{(1)}({\mathbb{P}}^{1},0,X)

classifying a
G
-bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}

and a section

\phi:{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{0\}\to\mathcal{E}\times^{G}X
, or equivalently, a
K
-reduction
\mathcal{E}_{K}
of

\mathcal{E}
on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
.
We have the uniformization isomorphism

(13.7)

Z\simeq LK^{(1)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(1)}|_{0}\simeq K({\mathbb{C}}[t^{-1}])\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}

in Section (
7.4
),
here
t
is the local coordinate at
0
.
The stratum
\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\subset{\operatorname{Gr}}
,
\lambda\in\Lambda_{S}^{+}
in Section
4.2
decends to
a stratum
Z^{\lambda}=K({\mathbb{C}}[t^{-1}])\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}\subset Z
, which is
smooth, locally closed sub-stack of
Z
and
the collection
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{Z^{\lambda}\}
forms a stratification of
Z
(see
[
GN1
, Section 3.4]
).
For example, the closed stratum
Z^{0}
is isomorphic to

Z^{0}\simeq K({\mathbb{C}}[t^{-1}])\backslash K({\mathcal{K}})/K({\mathcal{O}})\simeq\operatorname{Bun}_{K}({\mathbb{P}}^{1}).

Following
[
GN1
]
, we define the ind-stack
\operatorname{Hecke}_{Z}
of

generic Hecke modifications

to the ind-stack classifying data

({\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\underline{z},\tau)

where
({\mathcal{E}}_{i},\phi_{i})\in Z
,
\underline{z}\in\operatorname{Sym}\mathbb{(}{\mathbb{P}}^{1})
is a divisor on

{\mathbb{P}}^{1}
with support contained in
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{0\}
,
and
\tau
is an isomorphism of
G
-bundles

\tau:\mathcal{E}_{1}\simeq\mathcal{E}_{2}
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\underline{z}

compatible with the
K
-reductions of
\mathcal{E}_{1,K}
and
\mathcal{E}_{2,K}

on
{\mathbb{P}}^{1}\setminus\{0,\underline{z}\}
.
We have natural projection maps

Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{1}}}{{\longleftarrow}}\operatorname{Hecke}_{Z}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z\ \ \ \

given by

p_{i}(({\mathcal{E}}_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\underline{z},\tau))=(\mathcal{E}_{i},\phi_{i}).

We define a
smooth generic Hecke correspondence
to be any stack
Y
equipped with
smooth maps

Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1,Y}}}{{\longleftarrow}}Y\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{2,Y}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z

such that there exists a map
Y\to\operatorname{Hecke}_{Z}
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{1,Y}}
\scriptstyle{h_{2,Y}}
\textstyle{Z}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Hecke}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{Z}

We also have the ind-stack
Z\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}=\tilde{Z}\times^{G({\mathcal{O}})}{\operatorname{Gr}}
of Hecke modifications at
\{0\}

where
\tilde{Z}\to Z
is the
G({\mathcal{O}})
-torsor classiyfing the data

(\mathcal{E},\phi,\sigma)

where
(\mathcal{E},\phi)\in Z
and
\sigma
is a trivialization

\mathcal{E}|_{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}})}\simeq G\times\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{O}})

of
\mathcal{E}
on the formal neighborhood of
\{0\}
.
We have natural projection maps

Z\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{1}}}{{\longleftarrow}}Z\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h_{2}}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z\ \ \ \

Let
\operatorname{Perv}(Z)
be the category of perverse sheaves on
Z
.
Let
\operatorname{Perv}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(Z)\subset\operatorname{Perv}(Z)
the full subcategory
of perverse sheaves which are locally constant with respect to the stratification

{\mathcal{Z}}=\{Z^{\lambda}\}
.
We define a
generic Hecke-equivariant
perverse sheaf
on
Z
to a perverse sheaf
\mathcal{F}\in\operatorname{Perv}(Z)
on
Z
equipped with
isomorphisms

\phi_{Y}:h_{1,Y}^{!}\mathcal{F}\simeq h_{2,Y}^{!}\mathcal{F}

for every smooth generic Hecke correspondence
Y
, satisfying some uatural
conditions, see
[
GN1
, Section 3.2]
.
We denote by

\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}
the category of generic Hecke-equivariant
perverse sheaves on
Z
.
Since we assume
K
is connected, the condition of generic-Hecke equivariance is a property, not
addition structure of a perverse sheaf on
Z
by
[
GN1
, Proposition 3.5.2]
, we see that
the natural forgetful map

\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}\to\operatorname{Perv}(Z)
is fully-faithful
and induces an equivalence

\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}\simeq\operatorname{Perv}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}(Z)
.

Following
[
GN1
, Section 4.2]
,
consider the perverse Hecke action

\star^{p}:\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\times\operatorname{Perv}(Z)\to\operatorname{Perv}(Z)\ \ \ \ \ {\operatorname{IC}}_{V}\star^{p}\mathcal{F}:=\bigoplus_{i}{{}^{p}}H^{i}((h_{2})_{!}(\mathcal{F}\tilde{\boxtimes}{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}))

where
\mathcal{F}\tilde{\boxtimes}{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}\in\operatorname{Perv}(Z\tilde{\times}{\operatorname{Gr}})

is the twisted product of
\mathcal{F}\boxtimes{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}

with respect to the projections
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
.
Since the generic Hecke modifications commute with
Hecke modification at the pole point
\{0\}
, the perverse Hecke action descends to
a well defined functor

(13.8)

\star^{p}:\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})\times\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}\to\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}\ \

For any
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
, let

{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(Z)
be the intersection cohomology
complex of the stratum
Z^{\lambda}
(with constant coefficient).
By
[
GN1
, Proposition 3.5.1]
,
we have
{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}
.

Definition 13.9
.

[
GN1
, Definition 4.2.3]

Let
Q_{K}^{glob}\subset\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}
be the full
subcategory of
\operatorname{Perv}(Z)^{\operatorname{Hecke}}
whose objects are isomorphic
to direct summands of perverse sheaves appear in

{\operatorname{IC}}_{V}\star^{p}{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{0}}
for some
V\in\operatorname{Rep}(G^{\vee})
.

We have the
following description of
Q_{K}^{glob}
.

Proposition 13.10
.

[
GN1
, Theorem 1.2.1]

Q_{K}^{glob}
is a semi-simple abelian category
and every irreducible object is isomorphic to

{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}
for some
\lambda\in\Lambda_{A}^{+}
.

Remark 13.11
.

The proof of the proposition in
loc. cit.

is quite involved. We will give
an another proof of it using the results of the paper, see Corollary
13.15
.
In particular, we will show that in fact the irreducible objects of

Q_{K}^{glob}
are isomorphic to

{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}
,
\lambda\in\sigma(\Lambda_{T})
, confirming a conjecture in
loc. cit.
.

We now recall the construction of fusion product on

Q_{K}^{glob}
following
[
GN1
, Section 6.3]
.
Consider the base change

Z^{(2)}=QM^{(2)}(\mathbb{P}^{1},X)\times_{({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}}{\mathbb{C}}

of
QM^{(2)}(\mathbb{P}^{1},X)\to({\mathbb{P}}^{1})^{2}

along the diagonal embedding

{\mathbb{C}}\longrightarrow(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{2}
,
z\to(z,-z)

We have

Z^{(2)}|_{0}\simeq Z

and there is a
Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Z^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Z^{(2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\ \ i}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}
\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

The stratification
\{\mathcal{O}_{K}^{(2),\lambda_{\mathfrak{p}}}\}
of
{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)}

in Section
4.2
descends to a stratification

13.3.3.
The identification

The uniformization map (

Proposition 13.13
.

Proof.

.

It follows from the proposition above that

restricts to a functor

Theorem 13.14
.

The functor

restricts to
the horizontal tensor equivalence in a commutative

\textstyle{\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proof.

Since the functor

from the right, it restricts to a functor

.
We first show that

\Phi:Q_{K}\simeq Q_{K}^{glob}

By the description of

and (

and

{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}\simeq\operatorname{Im}({{}^{p_{cl}}}H^{0}((j_{Z}^{\lambda})_{!}(\omega_{Z^{\lambda}}[-\dim Z_{\lambda}]))\to{{}^{p_{cl}}}H^{0}((j_{Z}^{\lambda})_{*}(\omega_{Z^{\lambda}}[-\dim Z_{\lambda}])))

{\operatorname{IC}}_{K}^{\lambda}\simeq\operatorname{Im}({{}^{p}}H^{0}((j_{K}^{\lambda})_{!}(\omega_{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle]))\to{{}^{p}}H^{0}((j_{K}^{\lambda})_{*}(\omega_{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash\mathcal{O}_{K}^{\lambda}}[-\langle\lambda,\rho\rangle]))).

We shall show that
\Phi:=r^{!}[\dim K]:Q_{K}\simeq Q_{K}^{glob}
is a tensor equivalence.
We first show that for any

{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K},{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K}\in Q_{K}
, there is a canonical isomorphism

(13.15)

c:\Phi({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K})\simeq\Phi({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K})\star_{f}\Phi({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K})\simeq{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{1}}}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{2}}}.

called the
monoidal structure
on
\Phi
.

The uniformization isomorphism

gives rise to
a map

Consider the following Cartesian diagram

\textstyle{Z^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\scriptstyle{r^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}}}
\textstyle{Z^{(2)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r^{(2)}}
\textstyle{Z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{(K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}})^{2}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{(K({\mathcal{K}})^{(2)}\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}^{(2)})|_{{\mathbb{C}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K({\mathcal{K}})\backslash{\operatorname{Gr}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{\mathbb{C}}
\textstyle{\{0\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Proposition
8.11
implies that the map

is strongly pro-smooth and

implies that

\Phi({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K})=r^{!}[\dim K]i^{*}j_{*}({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\boxtimes{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}))\simeq

\simeq i^{*}j_{*}(r^{(2)}_{>0})^{!}({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\boxtimes{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}))[\dim K].

On the other hand, the same argument of proving (

) show that

(r^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})^{!}({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\boxtimes{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}[1]))[\dim K]\simeq{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{(2),\lambda_{1\cup 2}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}

where

is the

-complex on the stratum

in (

Thus we have

i^{*}j_{*}(r^{(2)}_{>0})^{!}(\mathcal{F}\boxtimes\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}))[\dim K]\simeq i^{*}j_{*}({\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{(2),\lambda_{1\cup 2}}}|_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}})[-1]\simeq\psi_{f}({\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{(2),\lambda_{1\cup 2}}})

\simeq{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{1}}}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{2}}}

Combining (
13.16
) and (
13.17
), we obtain the desired monoidal structure

c:\Phi({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K})\simeq i^{*}j_{*}(r^{(2)}|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{\times}})^{!}({\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{1}}_{K}\boxtimes{\operatorname{IC}}^{\lambda_{2}}_{K}\boxtimes\underline{{\mathbb{C}}}_{{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}}))[\dim K]\simeq{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{1}}}\star_{f}{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda_{2}}}

in (
13.15
).

Finally, we show that
\Phi
is compatible with associativity and commutativity constraints of

Q_{K}
and
Q_{K}^{glob}
.
Consider the following diagram of functors

(13.18)

\textstyle{\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\omega}
\textstyle{Q_{K}^{glob}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\omega^{glob}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Vect}}

where
\omega
and
\omega^{glob}
are the fiber functors.
The upper triangle and the outer square are
commutative.
We claim that the lower triangle is commutative, that is, there is natural isomorphism

\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi\simeq\omega
.
For this we observe that
the composition

\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}\to Q_{K}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi}}{{\to}}\operatorname{Vect}

is the fiber functor for
\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}
and, for any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{F}\in Q_{K}
, the
the monoidal structure for
\Phi
defines an isomorphism

\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi(\mathcal{F}\star_{f}\mathcal{F}^{\prime})\simeq\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi(\mathcal{F})\otimes\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}).

Thus by
[
Z2
, Lemma 3.3]
,
\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi:Q_{K}\to\operatorname{Vect}

has a structure of a fiber functor and
since
Q_{K}
is a neutral Tannakian category over
{\mathbb{C}}
,
the uniqueness of fiber functors in

[
DM
, Theorem 3.2]
implies that there is an isomorphism
\omega^{glob}\circ\Phi\simeq\omega
.

Let
R
,

B_{K}
,
B_{K}^{glob}
be the Hopf algebra corresponding to

\operatorname{Rep}G^{\vee}
,
Q_{K}
, and
Q_{K}^{glob}

under the Tannakian dictionary.
By
[
DM
, Proposition 2.16]
,
the commutative diagram
of functors in (
13.18
) gives rise to a maps

\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B_{K}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Phi^{*}}
\textstyle{B_{K}^{glob}}

where the vertical arrows are
surjective
morphisms of Hopf algebras
and the horizontal arrow
\Phi^{*}
is an
isomorphism
of co-algebras.
By
[
N2
, Lemma 9.2.1]
, the monoidal structure for
\Phi

implies that
\Phi^{*}
respects multiplication
and the surjectivity of the vertical arrows implies that

\Phi^{*}
is an isomorphism of
Hopf algebras.
The theorem follows.

∎

The following corollary follows immediately from

13.7
, Proposition

, confirming

, Conjecture 7.3.2.]
in the case of symmetric varieties.

Corollary 13.15
.

(1)
There is an isomorphism
H^{\vee}_{sph}\simeq H^{\vee}_{real}
.
In particular, the Weyl group of
H^{\vee}_{sph}
is isomorphic to the small Weyl group
of
X
.
(2) The
irreducible objects in
Q_{K}^{glob}
are
intersection cohomology sheaves
{\operatorname{IC}}_{Z^{\lambda}}

on the closures of strata

Z^{\lambda}
,
\lambda\in\sigma(\Lambda_{T})
, with
coefficients in trivial local systems.

Appendix A
Semi-analytic stacks

A.1.
Basic definitions

Recall that a subset
Y
of a
real analytic manifold
M

is called semi-analytic if
any point
y\in Y
has a open neighbourhood
U

such that the intersection
Y\cap U
is a finite union of sets of the form

\{y\in U|f_{1}(y)=\cdot\cdot\cdot=f_{r}(y)=0,g_{1}(y)>0,...,g_{l}(y)>0\},

where the
f_{i}
and
g_{j}
are real analytic functions on

U
. A map
f:Y\to Y^{\prime}
between two semi-analytic sets is called semi-analytic if
it is continuous and its graph is a semi-analytic set.

Let
\operatorname{Grpd}
be the
\infty
-category of spaces, which are often referred as
\infty
-groupoids.
Let
\operatorname{RSp}
be the site of semi-analytic sets where the coverings are
étale (=locally bi-analytic) maps
\{S_{i}\to S\}_{i\in I}
such that the map
\bigsqcup S_{i}\to S
is surjective.

Definition A.1
.

A semi-analytic pre-stack is a functor
{\mathcal{Y}}:\operatorname{RSp}\to\operatorname{Grpd}

and a semi-analytic stack is a
pre-stack which is a sheaf.

We will view any semi-analytic set as a semi-analytic stack via the Yoneda embedding.

A morphism
{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
between stacks is called representable if
for any morphism from a semi-analytic set

Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
, the fiber product
{\mathcal{X}}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}Y
is representable by a semi-analytic set. We say that a representable morphism
{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
has property
P if it has property P after base change along any morphism
from a semi-analytic sets.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a morphism of semi-analytic stacks.
One can associate its
\check{\operatorname{C}}
ech complex with terms

{\mathcal{X}}^{[n]}={\mathcal{X}}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}{\mathcal{X}}\times\cdot\cdot\cdot{\mathcal{X}}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}{\mathcal{X}}

the
(n+1)
-times fiber product of
{\mathcal{X}}
over
{\mathcal{Y}}
. When
f
is surjective, there is a natural isomorphism

(A.1)

\textstyle{{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq\operatorname{colim}(\cdot\cdot\cdot\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}^{[2]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}}^{[1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{X}})}

A semi-analytic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
is a semi-analytic space if for every
S\in\operatorname{RSp}
,

{\mathcal{Y}}(S)
is isomorphic to a set, that is, each connected component of
{\mathcal{Y}}(S)
is contractible.
Let
\Gamma\rightrightarrows Y
be a groupoid object
in the category of semi-analytic space.
The quotient stack

\Gamma\backslash Y
is defined as the colimit

(A.2)

\textstyle{\Gamma\backslash Y\simeq\operatorname{colim}(\cdot\cdot\cdot\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\times_{Y}\Gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y)}

A.2.
From stacks to
semi-analytic stacks

Let
F={\mathbb{R}}
or
{\mathbb{C}}
.
For any scheme
Y
over
F
of finite type, its
F
-points

Y(F)
is naturally a semi analytic set, denoted by
Y_{F}
.
For any strict ind-scheme
Y\simeq\operatorname{colim}_{i\in I}Y^{i}

over
F
, its
F
-points is naturally a semi-analytic space, denoted by

Y_{F}
.

In the paper, we will mainly consider
F
-stacks of the form

{\mathcal{Y}}\simeq Y/G
where
Y
is a strict ind-scheme acted on by a group ind-scheme

G
.
A surjective morphism
f:Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
from a strict ind-scheme
Y
to a
F
-stack

{\mathcal{Y}}
is called a

F
-surjective presentation if it induces a surjective map

Y(F)\to|{\mathcal{Y}}(F)|
on the set of isomorphism classes of objects.
Note that when
F={\mathbb{C}}
any surjective morphism is a
{\mathbb{C}}
-surjective
presentation.

Lemma A.2
.

Let
f_{1}:Y_{1}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
and
f_{2}:Y_{2}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be
two
F
-surjective presentations of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
Let
\Gamma_{i}=Y_{i}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}Y_{i}\rightrightarrows Y_{i}
be the corresponding
groupoid.
Then
there is a canonical isomorphism of semi-analytic stacks

\Gamma_{1,F}\backslash Y_{i,F}\simeq\Gamma_{2,F}\backslash Y_{2,F}.

Proof.

Let
Y=Y_{1}\times_{\mathcal{Y}}Y_{2}
and
\Gamma=Y\times_{\mathcal{Y}}Y

be the corresponding groupoid. Then
the natural map
\Gamma_{F}\backslash Y_{F}\to\Gamma_{i,F}\backslash Y_{i,F}
is an isomorphism. The lemma follows.

∎

Definition A.3
.

Given a stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
over
F
which admits a
F
-surjective presentation, we define the associated
semi-analytic stack to be

{\mathcal{Y}}_{F}:=\Gamma_{F}\backslash Y_{F}

where
Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is a
F
-presentation of
{\mathcal{Y}}
.

By the lemma above
{\mathcal{Y}}_{F}
is well-defined and the assignment

{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}_{F}
defines a functor from the category of
stacks over
F

which admit
F
-presentations to the category of
semi-analytic stacks.

Example A.4
.

Consider the case
F={\mathbb{R}}
.
Let
Y
be a
{\mathbb{R}}
-scheme and
G
be an algebraic group over
{\mathbb{R}}
acting
on
Y
. Consider the algebraic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}=G\backslash Y
.
Let
T_{1},...,T_{s}\in H^{1}(\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{C}}/{\mathbb{R}}),G({\mathbb{C}}))
be the isomorphism classes of

G
-torsors.
Define
G_{i}:=\operatorname{Aut}_{G}(T_{i})
and the
{\mathbb{R}}
-scheme

Y_{i}:={\operatorname{Hom}}_{G}(T_{i},Y)
. Note that

G_{i}
acts on
Y_{i}
and
the collection
\{G_{1},...,G_{s}\}
gives all the pure-inner
forms of
G
.
Consider the real algebraic stack
G_{i}\backslash Y_{i}
.
We have
G_{i}\backslash Y_{i}\simeq{\mathcal{Y}}
and the map

\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{s}Y_{i}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is a
{\mathbb{R}}
-surjective presentation. In addition,
the
{\mathbb{R}}
-surjective presentation above induces an isomorphism of semi-analytic stacks

\bigsqcup_{i=1}^{s}G_{i,{\mathbb{R}}}\backslash Y_{i,{\mathbb{R}}}\simeq{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathbb{R}}
.

One can regard real stacks as complex stacks with real structures and the discussion above has an obvious generalization to
this setting. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}
be a complex stack and let

\sigma
be a real structure on
{\mathcal{Y}}
, that is, a complex conjugation (or a semi-linear involution)
\sigma:{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
. Then
a surjective morphism
f:Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
of
{\mathcal{Y}}

is called a

{\mathbb{R}}
-surjective presentation if it satisfies the following properties.
(1) There is a real structure
\sigma
on
X
such that

f
is compatible with the real structures on
Y
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
.
(2) The map
f

induces a surjective map

Y({\mathbb{C}})^{\sigma}\to|{\mathcal{Y}}({\mathbb{C}})^{\sigma}|
. One can check that
Lemma
A.2
still holds in this setting,
thus
for a pair
({\mathcal{Y}},\sigma)
as above which admits a
{\mathbb{R}}
-surjective presentation, there is a well-defined
semi-analytic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathbb{R}}
given by

{\mathcal{Y}}_{\mathbb{R}}:=\Gamma({\mathbb{C}})^{\sigma}\backslash Y({\mathbb{C}})^{\sigma}
, where

Y\to{\mathcal{Y}}
is a
{\mathbb{R}}
-surjective presentation,

\Gamma=Y\times_{{\mathcal{Y}}}Y
is the corresponding groupoid (Note that

\Gamma
has a canonical real structure
\sigma
coming from

Y
and
{\mathcal{Y}}
).

A.3.
Constructible complexes

We will be working with
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear dg-categories. Unless specified otherwise, all dg-categories will be assumed cocomplete, i.e., containing all small colimits,
and all functors between dg-categories will be assumed continuous, i.e., preserving all small colimits.

For any semi-analytic set
S
,
we define
D(Y)=\operatorname{Ind}(D_{c}(Y))
to be the ind-completion
of the
bounded dg-category
D_{c}(Y)
of
{\mathbb{C}}
-constructible sheaves on

Y
. For any semi-analytic stack
{\mathcal{Y}}

we define
D({\mathcal{Y}}):=\underset{}{\operatorname{lim}}^{!}\ D(Y)

where the index category is that of semi-analytic sets equipped with a semi-analytic map
to
{\mathcal{Y}}
, and the transition functors are given by
!
-pullback.
Since we are in the constructible context,
!
-pullback admits a left adjoint, given by

!
-pushforward, and it follows that

D({\mathcal{Y}})=\underset{}{\operatorname{colim}_{!}}\ D(Y)
. In particular,

D({\mathcal{Y}})
is compactly generated.
For an ind semi-analytic stacks
{\mathcal{Y}}=\operatorname{colim}{\mathcal{Y}}_{i}
we denote by

D_{c}({\mathcal{Y}})\subset D({\mathcal{Y}})
the (non co-complete) full subcategory
consisting of complexes that are extensions by
zero off of substacks.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{Y}}
be a surjective morphism of semi-analytic stacks.
Then the isomorphism (
A.1
) induces an equivalence

(A.3)

\textstyle{D({\mathcal{Y}})\simeq\operatorname{lim}(D({\mathcal{X}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D({\mathcal{X}}^{[1]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D({\mathcal{X}}^{[2]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\cdot\cdot\cdot)}

Let
F={\mathbb{C}}
or
{\mathbb{R}}
.
For any
F
-stack
{\mathcal{Y}}
admitting a

F
-surjective presentation, we define

D({\mathcal{Y}})=D({\mathcal{Y}}_{F})
where
{\mathcal{Y}}_{F}
is the associated semi-analytic stack.
